26. (26 Sep) Table setup (table, decks, mixer) and crate (stand + case) are two movable groups. Desktop: drag table/legs or crate/stand, Shift-drag or wheel to rotate. XR: grab table edge or crate, thumbstick rotates/raises. Layout saved in localStorage ('vire.layout'); HUD "Reset layout".
27. (26 Sep) XR input: ray (controller trigger / hand pinch with nothing in reach) reuses the mouse code path; direct pinch/grip grabs tonearm, faders, pitch, knobs (twist about vertical), record edge (pick up), crate (slide to dig, lift to pull); fingertip pokes buttons and crate screen; fingertip resting on vinyl holds/scratches. Released record over a platter plays the side facing up. Controller and hand models load from the webxr-input-profiles CDN at runtime.
28. (26 Sep) Decided NOT to extract meshes/textures from the VRL APK (EntroPi's art, not licensed; matches the rule in "Folder"). Better look = improved procedural modelling and/or a licensed GLB through the PARTS factory.
29. (26 Sep) SUPERSEDES the ray part of 27: no at-a-distance manipulation in XR. Direct touch only (pinch / controller trigger or grip at the control, fingertip pokes). Thumbstick scrolls the crate when the hand is at the crate.
30. (26 Sep) Owner says VRL is free and on GitHub. Checked: EntroPi-Games/Vinyl-Reality-Lite-Releases hosts release builds only, no source or licence; Rekopy is the open-source part. Extraction still off unless EntroPi grants permission.
31. (26 Sep) Candidate deck model: mk2_1210_turntable_-_old.glb (Sketchfab, GRIP420, CC-BY-4.0: credit required). 5.7k tris, cm units, separate platter (table_sm), S-arm (arm_grp, pivot ~ rest_msh1), pitch fader (speed_sm), buttons. Body texture has a "Techno.ics Hartz" parody wordmark to paint out (#4). Not integrated yet.
32. (26 Sep) Songs: two sources, chosen on the start screen. PC = streamed; relative Rekopy paths or absolute file://localhost/D:/Music_Crates/... mapped to /vire-music/ (new server.js mount, plus audio MIME types). Headset = files imported once into the browser's private storage (OPFS; Quest Browser cannot read Quest folders directly), matched to XML locations by longest path tail then file name. Rekopy optional (useful for a smaller headset subset).
33. (26 Sep) GLB decks in use (web/models/turntable.glb, CC-BY credit on start screen). Wordmark painted out at load, pitch scale relabelled 0-16. Procedural decks remain as fallback if the GLB fails.
34. (26 Sep) Hands: 'real' = tracked hand mesh drawn as a passthrough cut-out (your camera hands show in front of the decks); '3d' = skin-tone hand mesh (always in full VR). Cheaper than 3D hands. Performance setting 'Recording-friendly' = 72 Hz, foveation 1, no shadows, 1024 record textures, AudioContext latencyHint 'balanced'.
35. (26 Sep) Record/platter touch rules (XR): label area = lift off, only after the hand rises 3 cm; grooves = scratch; platter rim = nudge (friction slows ~10%, pushing along speeds up). Edge grabs never remove the record. Crate: the selected record rides half out of its sleeve; grab its top half to pull. Controllers: thumbstick flick near a pitch fader = +/-0.05% (forward = faster), hold repeats. Controller touch tip = target-ray origin (controller front).
36. (26 Sep) Environment light setting: Studio / passthrough camera snapshot every 5 min (getUserMedia; Quest Browser has no WebXR camera-access) / user image, blended over the studio environment at an adjustable mix (default 50%).
37. (26 Sep) Look: records less glossy (clearcoat 0.04, anisotropy 0.45, env 0.4, rougher grooves, higher groove contrast). Mixer face = brushed aluminium canvas texture + bump (cut slots, printed legends, tick rings, screws). GLB slipmat hidden under a record and record raised 1.5 mm; camera near/far 0.05/30 to stop depth fighting in the headset.
38. (26 Sep) SUPERSEDES 26: no DJ table or crate pedestal. Turntable A, turntable B, mixer and crate are separate movable objects; two resizable flight cases (js/flightcase.js, look from owner's reference photo: black laminate, alu extrusions with rivets, lid-seam rails, ball corners, butterfly latches, side handles) act as plinths. Gear settles onto the case under its centre (else the floor); moving a case carries what sits on it; resizing lifts it. Resize handles only on the bottom corners/edges. Layout + case sizes saved in localStorage 'vire.layout2' (js/layout.js).
39. (26 Sep) SUPERSEDES the camera part of 36: passthrough camera snapshots removed (could disturb real-time recording). Environment light = Studio or a chosen image.
40. (26 Sep) Headset song storage kept only if the site's storage is persistent. Start screen shows persistence status + "Keep songs on this headset" (navigator.storage.persist()). Page is now an installable app (manifest.webmanifest, sw.js with no caching, icons) because installed apps get persistence most reliably. Storage is per address: always open the same URL (univdemo.a.pinggy.link, not the free random pinggy URL).
41. (26 Sep) Crate album jackets on the 10 sleeves nearest the selection only (pooled planes), cover art read from just the ID3 tag (HTTP Range / File.slice, 2 at a time). server.js now serves byte ranges (backup server.js.bak-before-range) and .webmanifest.
42. (26 Sep) SUPERSEDES the gravity part of 38: gear has no gravity; it stays where it is let go and snaps onto a case only if released within 6 cm of its top (then it rides with the case). Desktop: Shift+wheel while dragging changes gear height.
43. (26 Sep) SUPERSEDES the XR resize part of 38: flight cases are resized in XR with two hands on opposite sides (left/right = width, front/back = depth, both hands up/down = height). One hand = move. Desktop keeps bottom-corner drag.
44. (26 Sep) Passthrough hands: cut-out now built from the tracked joints (sphere per joint + capsule per bone + palm webbing, sized from each joint's measured radius x1.12 + 1.5 mm), replacing the generic skinned hand mesh that pinched/distorted. Hidden when tracking drops. Controller tip dot: 2.5 cm ahead of the target-ray origin, drawn on top of the controller model.
45. (26 Sep) Platter nudge = heavy platter model in the worklet ('touch' message): rate eases toward motor + 0.12 x (hand speed - motor) with a 0.45 s time constant, so the change scales with hand movement and contact time (holding the rim still for 1 s loses ~0.07 s). GLB decks: extra strobe rings and red strobe lamp removed (they looked like a second platter); nudging never depended on them.
46. (26 Sep) Grooves: normal map of concentric ridges at ~0.6 mm pitch (exaggerated so they resolve in a headset), deeper where the track is loud; wide turns on lead-in/run-out. Records: clearcoat 0, anisotropy 0.3, specular 0.4, env 0.25.
47. (26 Sep) GLB platter dots: the dotted platter body mesh rotates by the stroboscopic apparent angle (still at exact 33/45, drifting with pitch); with power off it just turns with the platter.
48. (26 Sep) Power dial (GLB polySurface15): twist ~20 deg in XR or click on desktop. Off = all deck lights out, START ignored, platter coasts down (0.14 rate/s, ~7 s) instead of the STOP brake.
49. (26 Sep) Throwable records: releasing a held record in XR away from a platter launches it with the hand's tracked velocity and spin; gravity, air drag, bounce (0.3), friction, settles flat. Lands on floor, cases, gear; an empty platter plays it (face-up side), the crate files it. Max 20 loose records. Loose records use 256 px maps without groove normals when on the floor or >30 cm from the eyes; any loose record can be picked up again.
50. (26 Sep) End of record: playhead continues into the run-out (lead-out spiral 3 s then locked groove), record keeps turning, soft click once per revolution. Tonearm grab zone = whole headshell (7 cm along the arm, 5 cm radius). Controller tip dot 0.5 cm ahead of the ray origin. Hand cut-out 5% smaller (x1.064 + 1 mm).
51. (26 Sep) Metal look: GLB metal_mat (tonearm, 45 adapter holder, buttons, trims) and flight-case chrome/extrusions/rivets now satin brushed steel (textures.js satinSteel: fine grain map + roughness/bump noise, roughness ~0.5, anisotropy), matching the owner's SL-1200GR2 reference photo instead of mirror chrome.
52. (26 Sep) Target light = the model's own pop-up (polySurface11, front right of the platter). The white glow sprite that sat on it is gone. Press/poke/click toggles: it rises over ~1 s and shines blue across the record (emissive bulb + glow + SpotLight), press again to sink flush. Off with the power dial.
53. (26 Sep) SUPERSEDES 47: platter dots turn with the platter again (the strobe illusion made the platter look stopped / not following pitch without a flickering lamp). Grooves drawn as distinct lines (bright wall every ~3 px at 2048) with a matching 2048 normal map; record textures always 2048 for decks/hand. Records: specular 0.28, env 0.12, rougher. Target lamp: bulb sits in the lamp head (uses the head's narrow extent, not the base), small glow, low grazing SpotLight aimed past the record centre. Controller tip: grip space (0, -0.01, -0.055). Thrown records sample 9 points (centre + rim) and rest on the highest surface; deck/mixer tops raised to clear platter/knobs. Metal: flight-case chrome/alu/rivets darker and env 0.35-0.4; in passthrough scene.environmentIntensity 0.28 so metal isn't self-lit.
54. (26 Sep) Target lamp: the model's lamp mesh (polySurface11) is split at load into lampBase (triangles below 15.36 model units, the round base plate) and lampTube; only the tube rises/sinks, the base stays on the deck. Both toggle the lamp.
55. (26 Sep) Target lamp lens: a 2.2 mm flat emissive disc set into the tube head's surface on the side facing the platter (head axis and surface found from the tube's own vertices), tiny glow; replaces the sphere bulb that stuck out of the tube.
56. (26 Sep) Records restyled after the owner's Vinyl Reality reference: matte dark vinyl (specular 0.18, env 0.08, anisotropy 0.1, roughness ~0.7-0.9), smooth-profile ridges every 1.8 mm, 5.5 mm bands of varying shade following the music, dark gap rings where the level drops; normal map at the same 1.8 mm pitch, low amplitude to avoid shimmer. Target lamp lens: turned 25 deg to its left, set flush into the tube wall (polygon offset), halo sprite removed.
57. (26 Sep) SUPERSEDES 46/53/56 groove styling. Root cause found: the groove rings in textures.js were drawn with radius in metres instead of pixels (tiny circles under the label), in both the colour and roughness maps, since the first build; the visible "grooves" were only lighting effects. Now: 8192-bin RMS+peak envelope per track; each pixel ring of the groove area = the envelope averaged over the bins it covers, normalised by 3rd/99.5th percentiles, lightness 5-35%. No synthetic ridges, no normal map. Records ~5% gloss (specular 0.05, env 0.05, anisotropy 0, roughness ~0.9). Target lamp: saturated blue lens (not tone-mapped, emissive 9), SpotLight 0x2f6bff intensity 7.
58. (26 Sep) Owner round after the groove fix. (a) Record gloss ~15%: specularIntensity 0.15, envMapIntensity 0.14, roughness scalar 0.86 over the groove roughness map (records and crate disc); grooves untouched (see "DO NOT BREAK" above). (b) Deck START/STOP and 33/45 buttons keep the plain satin brushed-steel caps: no green/orange emissive tint, no glow sprites; pressing dips the cap 1.5 mm for ~0.25 s. (c) Target lamp: controllers toggle it only with a trigger/grip press at the lamp head (2 cm); controller tips hovering never toggle it; bare hands still press it with a fingertip poke. (d) Loose records: root cause of records standing at 75-90 deg in the gear was surfaceUnder() overwriting the scratch vectors stepLoose used for the disc normal (and mutating its input point). Now surfaceUnder/supportUnder/stepLoose use separate scratch vectors; decks and mixer get height fields (1 cm cells) rasterised once from their own triangles (~16 ms), so a record rests on the highest knob/arm/lid under any of 25 sample points, lands exactly flat, and stacks on other loose records. An empty platter under the centre still auto-plays the record.
59. (26 Sep) Deck detail round. (a) Power dial rebuilt in place at load with 48 sides instead of the model's 16 (same radius/heights/materials; printed top re-mapped with an affine fit of the original cap UVs), ~340 tris. (b) Its dark window facing the platter is now the red strobe lamp lens (emissive, on with power). (c) Platter dots "strobing" in motion: two causes. The platter angle came straight from the worklet's ~54 Hz position reports, whose arrival jitter moved the platter by up to half a dot spacing per frame; the angle now advances smoothly each frame at the current speed and is steered gently toward the playhead (snaps if >0.35 rad off; hand scratching uses the hand rate with fast correction). And the headset refresh acts as a strobe (wagon-wheel effect), so the platter dot material now smears the dots along the rim by the angle turned in one frame, like the eye sees under steady light. (d) Red lamp light is computed in the platter shader: near the dial the dots are lit red with a frozen stroboscopic sample (100 flashes/s): rows calibrated -3.3 / 0 (big dots) / +3.3 / +6 % of the selected speed stand still at that pitch and drift either side; room light stays blurred. Texture u per radian of rim = 7.7209; dot pitch 0.25 u; sign verified headless. (e) Stylus rests 1 mm higher (recordTop + 0.001 in armDown).
60. (26 Sep) Baking, mixer look, microphone. (a) Turntable ambient occlusion baked without Blender (none installable here): tools/bake_ao.mjs grows near-planar charts, packs a 2048 atlas and ray-traces 64 cosine rays per texel (4 model units ~2.8 cm reach, floor at z=0) -> web/models/turntable_ao.png + turntable_ao.json (one UV1 per triangle corner, index order). Two bake sets: static body+platter+buttons+pitch cap+feet+arm rest; tonearm alone (it swings). Power dial (rebuilt at runtime), target lamp and slipmat are not baked and use a white texel. deck-glb.js un-indexes baked meshes, adds uv1, sets aoMap (channel 1, flipY false); the aomap chunk is patched so AO also takes up to 80 % off direct light. Re-run the baker if the GLB changes: node tools/bake_ao.mjs web/models/turntable.glb <out> then convert .raw (2048x2048 8-bit) to PNG. (b) Mixer knobs in the Vinyl Reality style: black rubber, knurled side (bump), flat satin top, white pointer line; printed scale = 11 dots over 300 deg. (c) SYNC, SPLIT, CUE and MIC are flat black rubber buttons with the legend cut out and lit by an LED (emissive mask), light leaking round the bezel, 1.2 mm press dip. (d) Microphone: MIC button (red LED, flickers with voice level) + MIC GAIN (0.5 = unity, max +12 dB) + PITCH (+-12 semitones, dead zone at centre) knobs in the centre column; meters moved down/tighter. Chain: getUserMedia (no AGC/noise suppression; echo cancelling on by default = "Feedback guard") -> 90 Hz high-pass -> gain -> mic-worklet.js delay-line pitch shifter (2 heads, 50 ms, sin^2 crossfade; bypass at ratio 1) -> on/off -> master bus (not on the crossfader). Start screen: mic device list (USB-C mics show up there), Allow microphone button, feedback guard checkbox. Needs HTTPS or localhost.
61. (26 Sep) Needle drag, crate screen, spare crate, leaning records. (a) Grabbing the tonearm while it plays keeps the stylus in the vinyl: dragging it across the record crosses grooves, each one jumping the playhead one revolution of audio (1.8 s) with a click, plus surface-scrape noise and weaker tracking as the drag speeds up (worklet 'needleDrag': hand moves spread over ~20 ms). Let go = it plays on from there; lift the hand ~2 cm or drag off the record to lift the needle. Desktop: drag the arm with the mouse (a plain click still cues / parks). Controllers buzz lightly while dragging. (b) Crate screen: flat matte LCD look (emissive picture on a rough dark surface, recessed in a black bezel), larger type, 8 rows. (c) Spare record crate: the owner's "Plastic Crate 02" GLB (Console Art Cybernetic, Sketchfab Standard licence; credit on the start screen) recoloured black, web/models/plastic_crate.glb, a movable stage item ('milk', default on the floor left of case A). Records tossed or dropped (desktop: click it while holding a record) into it stand on its floor leaning back 45 deg, more upright as it fills; they ride with the crate and can be picked up again. Procedural green milk crate is the fallback. (d) Records that come to rest beside anything 3 cm or more taller (deck, mixer, case side, crate) lean against it at 45 deg instead of lying flat.
62. (26 Sep) SUPERSEDES parts of 61. (a) Tonearm grab zone = the blue cartridge and its finger lift only (vertices of needle_system_msh on a 6 mm grid, reach 2 cm); nothing in front of the cartridge. While playing, the needle only lifts if the hand rises 7 cm (was 2 cm); anything less drags it across the record. (b) Spare crate uses real physics in its own frame instead of the 45-degree layout: floor/pile plane plus the four inner walls (disc extent along each wall normal = R*sqrt(1-n^2)). Arriving nearly flat (<35 deg) -> lies flat, stacking on flat records under its footprint; arriving on edge -> tips away from its contact point and leans square-on against the wall it falls toward if the disc can reach it (angle from acos(distance / diameter), later records lean on earlier ones), else falls flat. Taking a record out lets the flat ones above it drop. Desktop drops in from above the rim at a random tilt. Inner walls x 0.239, z 0.189, floor 0.008 (measured from the model). (c) MIC GAIN knob, now labelled LEVEL · DUCK: left half = mic level off -> full (+6 dB at centre); right half = mic stays full and, while MIC is on, the music ducks down to -24 dB at full right (duck node before the master; quick 60 ms down, 250 ms back up). The mic now joins after the duck.
63. (26 Sep) Record-load freeze: measured, the groove image is not the cause (2048 draw ~11 ms here vs ~3 ms at 1024); the Int16 conversion + loudness envelope on the main thread was. Moved to web/js/decode-worker.js (same maths as #57); the decoded channels are copied out in 1M-sample slices with yields between them and transferred to the worker. Headless, a 100 s track's longest main-thread stall went from ~142 ms to ~42 ms (the rest is the browser finishing decodeAudioData). Grooves stay 2048. Inline fallback kept in audio.js if a worker can't start.
64. (26 Sep) Deck button legends after the owner's SL-1200 reference photo: "START · STOP" printed on the start cap, "33" / "45" on the speed caps with a small LED bar to the right of the number (emissive only, no real light): red on the selected speed while the deck has power, dark grey otherwise. Dark print, because our caps are brushed steel. On controllers 33 and 45 now need a trigger/grip press at the button (like the target lamp); hovering the tip no longer switches speed. Bare-hand fingertip pokes still work. START still works by poke.
65. (26 Sep) Owner's slipmats: Slipmat_Left.png on deck A, Slipmat_Right.png on deck B. Both PNGs have the mat circle at (972, 1076) r 984.6 px (fitted); cropped to that circle, outside filled dark, resized to 1024 and saved as web/models/slipmat_left.jpg / slipmat_right.jpg (1.9 MB PNG -> ~170 KB JPEG q90 each; originals kept). The GLB slipmat disc (turn_plade) gets planar UVs from its own vertices at load; matte material (roughness 0.95). Still hidden under a record as before.
66. (26 Sep) (a) Crate lid screen restyled after the owner's photo of a broadcast monitor in a flight-case lid: lid now 28 cm tall with an aluminium edge frame, grey pick-foam lining (speckled canvas texture + bump), black velcro pads at the monitor corners, a slim dark monitor (29.6 x 18.5 cm matte LCD recessed 1.5 mm, 3 cm control strip with power button, input buttons, green LED and a small "ViRe" mark). XR poke on the screen now reads the screen size from its geometry. (b) Standalone Quest: sw.js is now network-first with an offline copy (cache vire-app-v1); the page also hands the worker every file it loaded before the worker took control, so one online visit is enough. Songs are never cached (range requests, /vire-music/, audio extensions skipped); imported songs live in OPFS. Tested headless: after one visit the app starts and builds both GLB decks and the crate with the network off. (c) deploy_web/ = snapshot of web/ for uploading to an HTTPS static host (no XML libraries, no check.html, no Collection junction). Rebuild it after code changes.
67. (26 Sep) sw.js also fetches the late-loaded files (deck-worklet.js, decode-worker.js, mic-worklet.js) when it installs, so offline start never misses them. Laptop-free via the existing laptop + Pinggy setup works if the Pinggy address stays the same (install the app from it once, import songs on the headset); a new random address = new origin = empty storage and no installed copy.
68. (26 Sep) Owner swapped back to the built-in milk crate: makeMilkCrate() is used directly (plastic crate GLB no longer loaded or credited; loadMilkCrate() kept, file still in web/models but removed from deploy_web for licence reasons). Material now dark green glossy moulded plastic: MeshPhysical 0x0c4a22, roughness 0.24, clearcoat 0.6 (roughness 0.15). XR grab box for it back to 19 x 19 x 29 cm.
69. (26 Sep) Drift audit. Both decks already run in ONE AudioWorklet on one sample clock, playheads advanced per sample in double precision, so clock-vs-clock drift between decks cannot happen (the "master-slave sample lock" is structural). Measured with tools/drift_test.py (real deck-worklet, OfflineAudioContext, 5 min, 128 vs 125 BPM synced): 0.00 ms drift with correct grids. The real remaining source is the beat grid: a Rekordbox BPM off by 0.03 gave 69 ms of slip in 5 min. Fix: decode-worker.js fine-tunes constant-tempo grids from the audio (onset curve at 5 ms hops; coarse search +-0.08 BPM / +-20 ms; then each beat's attack is located and a weighted least-squares line time = start + k*period is fitted with outlier rejection). Accepted only if the fit is tight (rms < 6 ms, >= 32 beats, within 0.1 BPM / 30 ms of Rekordbox); the first beat only moves if Rekordbox is > 4 ms off. Result: 69 ms -> 0.05 ms; a 0.004 BPM error 9.6 ms -> 1.5 ms; correct grids are left untouched. Variable-tempo grids are not changed. Original kept as track.gridOrig. SYNC's last glide step now sets the exact target pitch. Pitch moves by the DJ after SYNC (fader, thumbstick, platter touches) still change tempo by design (#2: no persistent lock).
70. (26 Sep) (a) Pulling a record from the crate starts fetching + decoding its playing side at once in the worker (like Vinyl Reality): grooves appear on the record in the hand (~0.6 s headless) and dropping it on a deck starts instantly (1 ms vs ~550 ms). Decoded PCM waits in a 2-entry cache (decodeCache) that loadSide takes from; groove envelopes are cached per side for the session (envCache, 32 KB each), so a record pulled again shows grooves immediately. Flipping in the hand pre-decodes the other side. (b) Crate jackets 1.4 mm thick (were 3.5 mm, looked like photo frames); covers sit 0.9 mm off the jacket. (c) Crate lid: foam, velcro pads and control strip removed; a 5 mm-bezel monitor fills the lid inside the aluminium frame; LCD 32.2 x 24.2 cm; canvas 1024 x 770, 10 rows. Screen = satin anti-glare: MeshPhysical base 0x030304 roughness 0.95 + clearcoat 0.4 (roughness 0.24), env 0.35, so the room lights show as a soft broad sheen like the owner's photo.
71. (26 Sep) Crate lid on hinges with a carry handle. models.js LID: hinge axis just behind the case's top back trim (crate y 0.306, z -0.179); two steel knuckles with fixed case leaves and L-bracket lid leaves; the lid panel sits 21 mm behind the hinge line so the shut lid clears the ball corners and never sweeps through the sleeves. Handle = black rubber grip on two satin-steel posts on the lid's outer face, on top when shut. Angle OPEN -0.42 (old pose) to CLOSED pi/2. main.js stepLid: XR grab the handle or the lid's free edge and swing it; let go and it falls by weight (past 0.12 rad forward of upright it drops shut, else back to the open stop, small bounce). Desktop: click the lid = open/close (~0.8 s), drag up/down = swing, key O. While shut: no digging, pulling, crate-screen presses or sleeve wheel; the selected record sinks back into its sleeve; thrown records rest on the lid. State saved in localStorage 'vire.crateLid'. Lid covers 29 of 34 cm depth (monitor size from #70 kept). Backup of the three files in backups/pre-lid-hinge/.
72. (26 Sep) SUPERSEDES the lid geometry in 71. Lid = shallow laminate tray exactly the case's size (walls on the case's wall lines, 16 mm alu edges centred on the same corner lines, rim rails matching the case's top trims, corner posts), so it closes flush on the rim. Hinge axis = outer back edge of the seam (crate y 0.306, z -0.178); all lid parts lie outside the seam, so it never sweeps through the records. Chrome ball corners moved from the case top to the lid's four outer corners. Monitor recessed in the tray: LCD 32 x 30 cm, crate screen canvas now 1024 x 960 with 13 rows. Handle centred on the outer face. Backup in backups/pre-lid-flush/.
73. (26 Sep) Crate search. Scope = whole collection (all records), every field: title, artist, genre, key and BPM; all words must match ('128' = within 1 BPM, '124-130' = range, a key like 'am' / 'f#m' / '8a' matches the key exactly or text). Ranked title-prefix > word start > anywhere; missing files last. Results replace the crate list (LCD + sleeves) until cleared (clear button, header tap or playlist arrows). LCD: search bar under the header with field, keyboard, mic (emoji) and clear buttons; tapping the field or the keyboard button opens an on-screen keyboard on the LCD (5 rows, ~3 cm keys, poke / controller tip / mouse; live results; rows shrink 12 -> 6 while it is open). Mic = focus a real <input> so the Meta system keyboard (with its voice dictation) can appear; in the headset this needs a user gesture, so xr.js checks the mic button inside the selectstart handler (trigger press or pinch at it); a plain fingertip poke can't open it and shows a hint on the LCD. Desktop: '/' or the mic button focuses the same field (top centre), Enter = done, Esc on empty = clear. UNTESTED on Quest: whether Quest Browser shows its keyboard for a focused input during an immersive session. Backup in backups/pre-search/.
74. (26 Sep) Owner fixes. (a) Crate lid flicker (z-fighting): the LCD sat 0.5 mm in front of the monitor's back box of the same size; the back box now ends 4.5 mm behind it and the LCD material has polygonOffset (-2, -4). Also removed every coplanar overlap in the lid tray: outer skin inset 0.5 mm inside the alu edges, tray walls stop 1 mm short of the rim face, side edges/rails run past the corners and the end ones butt into them, corner posts only fill the gap between edge and rail. Rule for new geometry: never let two faces share a plane where both are visible. (b) Channel fader caps 2% wider (0.016 -> 0.01632 m). (c) Crossfader cap 2% taller (scale.y 1.02). (d) Mixer meter LEDs 3% less emissive glow (set() values x0.97). Backup in backups/pre-zfight/.
75. (26 Sep) Owner follow-up to 74: meter LEDs another 5% less emissive (set() x0.97 x0.95 = 0.9215); channel fader caps another 5% wider (0.016 x1.02 x1.05 = 0.01714 m).
76. (26 Sep) SUPERSEDES 75. Meter LEDs another 10% less emissive (set() x0.97 x0.95 x0.9 = 0.83). Channel fader caps widened to nearly touch the printed fader scale: base 19.6 mm, top plate 20 mm (half 10 mm) vs the long ticks starting 10.8 mm from the fader centre (scale at +-13 mm, long ticks 18 px at 4129 px/m).
77. (26 Sep) Knob hard stops. Bug: XR knob twist mapped the total twist since the grab through wrap(), so past 180 deg it jumped to -180 and the value flipped between min and max (the controller-ray twist path had the same wrap). Now both are incremental: each frame's twist (capped at 0.6 rad to ignore yaw flips when the hand points up/down) is added to the current value, and setMix clamps it, so turning past an end does nothing and turning back leaves the stop at once. Backup in backups/pre-knobstop/.
78. (26 Sep) SUPERSEDES the thumbstick direction in 35: thumbstick near a pitch fader now flicks forward = -0.05 %, back = +0.05 % (hold repeats).
79. (26 Sep) Glass plate over the mixer readout screen: plane 2 mm in front of it (19.4 x 7.9 cm), black MeshPhysical (roughness 0.06, env 1.4) with additive blending so it only adds reflections and never dims the display; no transmission; raycast disabled.
80. (26 Sep) (a) Mixer screen glass more reflective: roughness 0.03, env 3.2, clearcoat 1 (still additive, never dims the display). (b) Neon sign prop, web/js/neon.js, in the style of the owner's neon-arrow reference: the Dirrogate circle logo (outer ring with its top-left gap + dot, inner ring with its bottom-right gap + dot, the Gemini glyph's outer contour and inner rectangle) rebuilt from the logo's stroke centrelines as 10.4 mm tubes, 60 cm across at scale 1. Tube = unlit shader, white-hot facing you and deep red at its edges; two additive halo shells (2.4x and 5x tube radius, fading at their silhouettes) with rounded halo ends at the ring gaps; no post-processing. Tubes stand 3 cm off a dark gunmetal backing disc on small posts. Two clamps: a black clip strap across both tubes of each centre bar (the glyph's pillars) with saddles over the tubes, legs to the backing and screws with red-lit rims. One red PointLight 45 cm in front (no shadows). Movable stage item 'neon' (default centred behind case A, 1.45 m up, facing the DJ). Resize: in XR grab one centre bar with each hand (controller or pinch) and pull apart / push together (scale 0.3-4, about the centre); a second hand anywhere else on the sign is ignored. Desktop: mouse wheel over it = +-5%. Size in localStorage 'vire.neonScale'; Reset layout sets it back to 60 cm. Backup of main.js / xr.js in backups/pre-neon/.
81. (26 Sep) Neon sign: backing disc and its tube posts removed (owner); the clamps keep their straps and saddles, screws with lit rims now sit on the strap ends. Glass merged into 3 meshes (core + 2 halos): 17 draw calls / ~20k triangles for the whole sign (was 44 / 29k). Light on/off goes by intensity, never .visible (changing the light count recompiles every lit shader = a hitch). Cost of the sign on Quest: the PointLight adds one light to the per-pixel loop of every lit material in the scene (8 lights total now; the records and deck bodies are MeshPhysical, the priciest); the additive halos cost fill-rate only where the sign covers the view; triangles/draw calls are minor. Owner asked whether record groove preloading changed: it did not (no edits to predecode / decode-worker / drawRecordSide / envCache since #70; diffed against backups/pre-lid-hinge). Any slower feel would come from frame time (neon light, bigger 1024x960 crate screen texture since #72).
82. (26 Sep) Lighting pass (owner). (a) Neon sign: PointLight removed; glow is emissive tube + additive halos only, halos raised (inner 0.55 -> 0.8, outer 0.22 -> 0.34, outer falloff 1.2 -> 1.1). (b) Studio: environment-driven. HemisphereLight fill and the blue / purple accent PointLights removed; key DirectionalLight 1.6 -> 1.0 (still casts shadows outside Recording-friendly mode); scene.environmentIntensity 0.55 -> 1.0 (EnvLight default), so the studio room or the owner's chosen image (blended at the Environment mix setting) now gives the ambient light and colour. Passthrough keeps 0.28. Punctual lights left: key + the two target-lamp SpotLights (intensity 0 until a lamp is raised; they still count in the shader loop). The old purple/blue cast on the flight cases is gone unless the chosen image carries it. Backup of main.js in backups/pre-envlight/.
83. (26 Sep) Neon sign LOD. THREE.LOD with two levels: full geometry (tubes + clamps, ~20k tris, 17 draw calls) closer than 1.83 m (6 ft) x sign scale, beyond that one flat quad (2 tris, one additive layer) in the sign's own plane (not camera-facing: the sign is flat, so it stays correct in stereo), hysteresis 8%. The quad's texture is baked at load by bakeNeonImpostor(): the lit sign rendered once head-on with a distant orthographic camera into a 1024 px mipmapped render target (~5 MB GPU), shown raw with additive blending (black = nothing; the dark clamps drop out at distance). Headless: impostor and full sign look the same even at 0.9 m. Threshold scales with the sign's size (setLodScale). Context: whole scene ~71k triangles / ~350 draw calls; on Quest 3 the draw calls and per-pixel shading matter more than the sign's triangles.
84. (26 Sep) Performance pass + one flight case. (a) SUPERSEDES the case part of 38: flight case 2 (under the record crate) removed; one case left. The crate defaults to the floor (0.98, 0, 0.12) and, having no gravity, stays wherever it is let go (floor, on the milk crate, or on real furniture in passthrough). Old saved layouts: if the crate was standing on case 2 it is moved to the floor once. (b) Draw calls 354 -> 142 (headless count, no records loaded). web/js/merge.js mergeStatic(root, skip): one mesh per material + vertex layout + interaction tag (move/resize/lid/control/platterOf) + shadow flags, baked into root space; moving parts and controls stay separate. Used on: flight case (52 -> 19, redone on each resize), crate body and lid (64 -> 22), mixer static body, GLB deck bodies (44 -> 35 each; the rest are moving parts), neon clamps (17 -> 6). Mixer knobs are empty proxy groups (u.controls[id], still turned by updateMixVisual) drawn by 3 InstancedMeshes (skirt, body side+top, pointer; u.syncKnob copies the pose); hits resolve through hitTarget(hit) (instanceId -> proxy). Meter LEDs: 2 InstancedMeshes (caps + tilted flat glow) behind the same meters[c][i].userData.set(v) interface. Mixer 122 -> 40. Height fields include the instanced knobs. (c) Physical -> Standard where the extra layer was invisible: flight-case aluminium and satin chrome, crate hinge steel, neon screws (satinSteel({physical:false})). Physical kept on records, mixer face/glass, crate screen, deck metal, milk crate. 27 physical meshes left. (d) Not done: Lambert for matte surfaces, because in three.js it does not take ambient light from scene.environment (the owner's image) and would need a LightProbe. (e) Bug found: three's raycaster hits hidden objects; the decks' unused 1 m target-lamp glow sprites sat invisibly over the mixer and could swallow clicks. castRay now skips hidden objects and those sprites don't raycast. Backups in backups/pre-merge/.
85. (26 Sep) Power dial wind-up: switching power back on while the platter is still coasting (rate > 1% of speed) and the motor was running at switch-off re-engages the motor, so the platter spins back up (worklet ACCEL) from its current speed. From a dead stop, START is still needed.
86. (26 Sep) (a) Neon glow up: inner halo 0.8 -> 1.2 (falloff 1.7), outer halo 0.34 -> 0.55 (falloff 0.95) and wider (6.5x tube radius on the rings, 4.2x on the glyph, whose 23 mm bends creased a fatter shell), more halo segments, tube edges full red; impostor extent 0.335 m. (b) Random flicker: every 30 s - 2 min (uniform) a burst of 3-9 quick dips to a faint glow (30-150 ms each), 40% chance of one longer dark gap (0.35-0.85 s), then steady; drives uOn on the tube, both halos and the far LOD quad; screw rims dim with it. neon.userData.update(dt) each frame, flickerNow() for testing. (c) SUPERSEDES the release rule of 71: the crate lid is a friction hinge. Let go within 20 deg of shut = drops closed; opened past 100 deg = falls back to the open stop (~114 deg); anywhere between stays where it was left. The resting angle is saved ('vire.crateLid' = 'open' | 'closed' | radians). Records stay reachable only with the lid past ~73 deg open. Desktop click still toggles fully open / shut. (d) Lid fully shut: its carry handle (the rubber grip) picks up and moves the whole crate (XR: grab it; desktop: drag the grip, Shift-drag turns). With the lid open, the same grip swings the lid as before.
87. (26 Sep) Bug: with the record crate standing on the milk crate, a record let go over it (e.g. after pulling a search result in XR) landed inside the milk crate below: surfaceUnder() let the milk crate override the record crate whenever the point was inside both footprints. Now the milk crate only wins if its rim is higher than the record crate's rim at that point.
88. (26 Sep) More milk crates. A green crate-with-plus icon in the lid screen's search bar (left of the keyboard button) spawns another milk crate, up to 6 in all. It grows in on the floor at the first free spot on rings round the record crate (front first, clear of the flight case, the record crate and other milk crates). Extra crates are stage items milk2..milk6 (saved with the layout; which exist in 'vire.milkKeys'), each with its own records and physics (loose entries carry L.milk; surfaceUnder/enterMilk/milkDrop/pileTop/resettleMilk take the crate; the highest crate under a point takes a record). Removal = toss away (owner's choice): let go of a crate moving faster than 1.2 m/s in XR and it flies (ballistic) and lands on the floor; an extra crate that ends up more than 3 m from the decks, thrown or dragged, is deleted with the records in it (they go back to the library). The original milk crate is never deleted. Reset layout removes all extras.
89. (26 Sep) (a) Thrown milk crates: an extra crate released faster than 1.2 m/s vanishes (shrinks out in 0.2 s, deleted with its records) as soon as it is more than 0.6 m, horizontally, from the user's head; landing first keeps it. Dragging away (desktop) still uses 3 m from the decks. (b) Mixer readout tilted back 15 deg more (20 -> 35 deg) with its stand. Fake glass refraction in the screen's own shader (onBeforeCompile, no extra pass): 4 mm glass (n 1.5) shifts the picture with viewing angle, a 5 mm bevelled edge bends it inward with slight colour split, and a thin glint on the bevel at grazing angles. Glass pane: clearcoat removed, reflection strength via specularIntensity 0.4. Finding: with scene.environment set, three.js ignores material.envMapIntensity (it uses scene.environmentIntensity), so the 3.2 from #80 never did anything; since #82 raised the environment to 1.0 the glass washed out the readout once tilted. (c) Centre buttons, top to bottom: MIC, LOCK (the SYNC button renamed, now the same 24 x 11 mm as SPLIT), SPLIT. The S key still triggers it.
90. (26 Sep) Power-on wind-up (#85) is gentler: while the platter is still coasting, power back on engages the motor at WINDUP = 1.2 rate/s instead of the normal 6 (about 0.6 s from 30 % speed back to full, ~0.2 s from 75 %). START/STOP and power-off clear it; it ends when the platter reaches speed.
91. (26 Sep) Full backup: backups/ViRe_backup_2026-09-26_2050.zip (139 files, 31.6 MB): web/ (without the Collection music junction), deploy_web/, CLAUDE.md, tools, renders, source images/GLBs, Rekordbox XML and the per-change backups/pre-* folders. Left out: the Vinyl Reality APK zip and its unzipped folder (reference only), _to_delete, older backup zips.
92. (26 Sep) Record gloss. Measured: every record in the library is single-sided (720/720), so every B side is blank, and a blank side's groove area was never painted in the roughness map, leaving it at roughness 0: fully smooth, the gloss the owner liked. A cut side was ~0.82 roughness (~18% gloss). Now (textures.js drawRecordSide, roughness map only; the colour/waveform map is untouched): blank groove area explicitly 0; cut sides ~0.40 across rim, lead-in, grooves and run-out = ~60% of the blank side's gloss (gloss = 1 - roughness), grooves 0.35 (quiet) to 0.45 (loud) using the same percentile-normalised envelope as the colour map, so the waveform still shows in the sheen. Label stays 0.67. Both sides keep specularIntensity 0.15; if the A side still reads too matte in the headset, raise its specularIntensity next (not rings/clearcoat, see DO NOT BREAK). Backup in backups/pre-gloss/.
93. (26 Sep) SUPERSEDES the cut-side values in 92: A (cut) sides now 95% of the blank side's gloss: roughness rim 0.06, lead-in and run-out 0.04, grooves 0.03 (quiet) to 0.07 (loud). Label unchanged (0.67). Colour/waveform map untouched.
94. (26 Sep) Label relief (bump map), 256 px (owner). textures.js labelBumpMap(): covers only the label + 12 % (read through a uv1 set that scales the disc UVs up round the centre); printed ink edges proud (high-pass of the label's own lightness: blurred minus sharp, so text/lines emboss and flat colour stays flat, works for ID3 cover art too), fine paper grain, raised pressing ring at 82 % of the label radius, small step down at the label edge, lip round the spindle hole. Outside the label it is exactly flat (128), so the grooves get no relief (DO NOT BREAK holds). bumpScale 4 (1.6 was invisible). Record3D.setBump(on): on within 0.65 m of the eyes, off past 0.8 m (hysteresis), set every frame for deck records, the held record and loose records; off = bumpMap removed from the material entirely, and always off for low-detail (LOD) records. Maps built on first need (~1 ms each), rebuilt when the label changes. Cost: 3 extra texture reads per record pixel while near, ~0.35 MB per side (with mips). Backup in backups/pre-labelbump/.
95. (26 Sep) Full backup: backups/ViRe_backup_2026-09-26_2159.zip (same scope as #91: web/ without the Collection junction, deploy_web/, CLAUDE.md, tools, renders, sources, backups/pre-* folders; no APK, no older zips).
96. (27 Sep) Fake light instead of real lights / shadow maps (web/js/fakelight.js), both on the start screen. (a) Neon wall glow: neon.js neonGlowTexture() draws the logo's own centrelines thick in red and blurs them (256 px, spans +-0.55 m at scale 1); one quad, child of the sign, so it moves and scales with it and flickers with the tubes (neon.userData.level). Full VR / desktop: a dark studio back wall (rig z -0.6, shown only while the glow is on) receives it when the sign faces it from <= 60 cm; further away = wider and dimmer (0.75 / (1 + (d/0.12)^2)). Passthrough: drawn 5 cm behind the tubes, so hang the sign ~5 cm off the real wall. Modes: 'add' (adds light, leaves framebuffer alpha untouched: shows over passthrough only if the Quest compositor treats the layer as premultiplied - to verify on the headset) and 'tint' (writes alpha: certain to show, slightly dims the camera image where faint). (b) Blob shadows: soft quads under decks, mixer, record crate, milk crates, the flight case and records in the air or lying about; each lands on the highest known surface under it (floor, case, record/milk crate) and grows, softens and fades with height, gone at 35 cm (so gear on unknown real furniture gets none). (c) Shadows setting: Blob (default) / Real-time (Best look mode only) / Both / Off; with Blob the shadow map is off everywhere, including the desktop. (d) Neon far LOD now only beyond 3 m x scale (was 6 ft). (e) Start screen now scrolls when the window is short (the Desktop button had become unreachable). Backup in backups/pre-fakelight/.
97. (27 Sep) Owner's headset test of #96: 'adds light' wall glow works in passthrough; the sign looked like fat extruded capsules; no blob shadows at all. Fixes: (a) Neon halo is now flat, like the photographed neon arrow: the two 3D halo tube shells (2.4x / 6.5x tube radius) are gone. They had real depth, so in stereo they read as translucent capsules, and their AdditiveBlending wrote alpha 1, which also hid passthrough behind them. Now: thin glass tubes (10 sides, white-hot core, red edges) + one halo quad 6 mm behind the tube centres (neonHaloTexture: 512 px over +-0.40 m, the logo's centrelines drawn as a tight hot rim, a ~1 cm red spill and a faint ~3 cm spill, 'lighter' compositing) + the wall glow. All neon glow uses lightBlend() (additive, framebuffer alpha untouched), including the far LOD quad. (b) Blob shadows were invisible because MeshBasicMaterial.alphaMap reads the green channel and the blob textures were drawn black on transparent (alpha map = 0 everywhere). Now white on black. Rule: other additive glows in the app (LED glow sprites, button glows) still use AdditiveBlending and will block passthrough behind them the same way; switch them to lightBlend if that shows.
98. (27 Sep) Owner's headset test of #97 on a light beige wall. (a) Blob shadows under records removed entirely (they glitched); records get none. (b) Gear blobs 15% darker: flight case / milk crates 0.52, record crate 0.63, other gear 0.58. (c) Neon wall glow was a near-opaque orange-red disc filling the logo on light walls. Now: glow and halo textures are tighter and deeper red (dark-red outer spill, rgb 200,6,2 core); wall-glow spread 1 + d*1.2; intensity 0.5 (was 0.75). Both glow modes in fakelight.js now use premultiplied blending (One, OneMinusSrcAlpha). The 'add' mode writes alpha 0.6*a, so the wall/passthrough behind darkens slightly under the glow. Rule: pure additive red on a light surface always reads salmon/orange; a deep red there needs some dimming of what is behind.
99. (27 Sep) Owner feedback on #98. (a) Neon glow more saturated red: wall-glow texture rgb 165,0,0 / 235,0,0, halo mid 215,0,0 and core 255,24,14 (no green/blue in the spill); the wall-glow 'add' mode dims 75% of alpha (was 60%). (b) Owner is turning blob shadows off in settings: random visual glitches on Quest 3 with them on. Cause not found yet. (c) Label relief showed only the pressing ring; photo art barely embossed because the ink term was one narrow high-pass (2 px blur, +-26). labelBumpMap now uses three terms: fine edges (1.5 px blur, gain 2.2, +-30), mid-scale shapes (7 px blur, gain 0.9, +-28) and tone (dark areas up to +14). Darker ink stands proud; white text reads slightly engraved. (d) Cut side (A) gloss 8% lower: gloss 0.95 -> 0.874 of the blank side, i.e. roughness +0.076 on the vinyl areas (A_DULL in drawRecordSide). The label and groove rules are unchanged.
100. (27 Sep) Owner: label relief too strong when the label is not in direct light, and it faded as the record came closer (right only at arm's length). Cause, from the r169 shader: three's bumpMap (dHdxy_fwd / perturbNormalArb) takes the height step across one SCREEN pixel and normalizes the surface derivatives. So the tilt scales with texels per pixel: strong far away, weak close up. It is not a bug in our height map, and a 512 map would not fix it (twice the texels per pixel = the fade gets worse at the same distance, plus 4x memory and build time). Fix: labelBumpMap now bakes the same height field into a tangent-space NORMAL map (slope per texel, K = 4 = the old bumpScale 4 at arm's length, where one texel is about one Quest 3 pixel). The relief is now fixed on the label at any distance, and it is cheaper (1 texture fetch instead of 3). Steep slopes go through a soft knee (v / (1 + |v|/0.3)). Before, ink edges and the ring tilted the normal ~20-25 deg into bright/dark parts of the environment, which is what looked exaggerated in shade. The map is flat (128,128,255) outside the label; grooves still get no normal or bump map (DO NOT BREAK rule intact). models.js sets mat.normalMap (uv channel 1) instead of bumpMap.
101. (27 Sep) Owner: synced tracks sound out of sync even though phase and BPM readouts match. BPM source: the Rekordbox XML (TEMPO grid / AverageBpm), never the MP3's ID3 tag. Tested 8 of the owner's real MP3s (read-only folder grant to D:\Music_Crates\VinylRealityLite_Crate\Collection), decoded in headless Chromium at 48 kHz. Kick-band attacks were phase-folded against each grid, first third vs last third. Results: Rekordbox's grids hold steady (e.g. Eve 140.00: 9 / 9 ms, Track II 123.00: 32 / 32 ms). #69's refineGrid did not reach its fine least-squares fit on any of them; it used the coarse fallback every time. That fallback pinned the first beat to the -20 ms search edge and nudged exact BPMs (140 -> 140.012, 123 -> 123.008, 123.83 -> 123.89), adding 10-48 ms of slip per track. So readouts and phase lined up the wrong grids. Fix: decode-worker.js REFINE_GRID = false; the Rekordbox grid is used as-is (refineGrid kept for reference; applyGridFix is a no-op on null). Remaining: the kick sits a different distance after Rekordbox's grid line per track (~10-35 ms in this sample, detector bias included), so a small flam between some pairs is Rekordbox grid placement; fix those in Rekordbox. Songs staged for the test were deleted from the container afterwards.
102. (27 Sep) SUPERSEDES the BPM source in #2/#8/#12/#69 and the phase nudge in LOCK. Owner: "Priority should be ID3 bpm listed in the mp3 track and the Lock (sync) should just meet the two tracks bpms half way... before rekordbox, vinyl DJs just used bpm and ear." Now: (a) id3.js reads TBPM/TBP (40-250, comma decimals ok). useID3Bpm() runs wherever a tag is read: the crate's cover-art tag read and the full file read when a record is pulled. A track with an ID3 BPM uses it (track.bpm = ID3, track.rbBpm = Rekordbox, bpmSrc 'id3'), and the record's crate BPM updates with it. Without one, Rekordbox's BPM is used as before (grid marker at the playhead). (b) LOCK = only the 0.4 s meet-halfway pitch glide; the worklet phase nudge is no longer sent. Beat-matching the phase is the DJ's ear. (c) The Rekordbox beat grid is only used for the beat flashes. Tested headless with the owner's Eve (ID3 140) and Track II (ID3 123): pitches +3% / -2%, LOCK -> both 132.37 BPM (-5.45% / +7.62%), 0 phase nudges. Backup: backups/pre-101b.
103. (27 Sep) Owner: Rockstar and See You Again (both 80 BPM) - the mixer's beat dots sometimes count 1-2-3-1, and after LOCK look like 3/4 vs 4/4. Cause: library.js beatAt() ignored each Rekordbox TEMPO marker's beat number (Battito) and restarted the bar at beat 1 on every marker. Rockstar has about 30 extra markers between 61 and 104 s (all 80.00 BPM, consistent with one continuous 4/4 grid but starting on beats 2/3/4), so it counted 1-2-3-1 / 1-2-1-2 there. See You Again has a clean grid. Fix: beatAt = (Battito - 1) + beats since the marker. Simulated over Rockstar 55-110 s: 19 out-of-order steps before, 0 after. Pitch and LOCK never touched the count; the bad stretch just happened to coincide. Since #102 LOCK does not align phase, so the two decks' dots line up only once the DJ beat-matches by ear.
104. (27 Sep) Owner. SUPERSEDES the lift zone in #35. (a) Controllers: before this, trigger and grip did the same thing everywhere. Now on the turntables (tonearm, power dial, platter rim, record) only the TRIGGER acts. The grip (middle finger) still grabs and moves everything else. A grab ends only when the button that started it is released. Hands (pinch / fingertip) unchanged. (b) Record lift-off zone = the plain vinyl ring from LABEL + 3 mm to LABEL + 2 cm (50-70 mm radius), then lift 3 cm as before. The label itself (r < LABEL + 3 mm) does nothing; it is kept free for the planned spindle twist. Scratch = r > LABEL + 2 cm, and the hand-fingertip scratch zone matches. (c) Needle auto-drop: carrying the raised arm inward, once the stylus crosses from outside the disc edge (EDGE + 3 mm) into the lead-in, the arm leaves the hand, settles on the first groove (REC.OUT) and lowers onto it (last quarter of the drop at half speed), cue 0:00. It only triggers when entering from outside, so swinging the arm back out never drops it. Later hand moves / release on that grab are ignored (arm.auto). Tested headless on Eve: dropped at yaw -0.468, needle down at 0.00 s. Backup: backups/pre-104.
105. (27 Sep) Spindle twist (owner). XR: trigger (or hand pinch) on the label / spindle (r < LABEL + 3 mm) and twist about the vertical. The record moves by exactly the twisted angle, 1:1 like a real spindle: one turn = 1.8 s of audio, 10 deg = 50 ms, clockwise from above = forward. The motor keeps running at pitch-fader speed, so tempo is untouched and nothing drifts after. Haptic tick every 5 ms of shift; the per-frame twist is capped at 0.6 rad against wrist-yaw flips. Worklet: new 'shift' message spread over ~20 ms (DRAG_K), like the needle drag. The platter visual follows fast for 250 ms after a twist. Desktop: mouse wheel over the label, 5 ms a notch (Shift: 1 ms). Also confirmed (owner): the #104 needle auto-drop works with the platter already spinning; the needle lands on the first groove and the DJ then cues the first kick by hand. Tested headless: drop while spinning OK; twist +50 ms -> +51.9 ms measured, -20 ms -> -18.5 ms, rate stays 1.000. Backup: backups/pre-105.
106. (27 Sep) Owner: spindle twist only on the spindle geometry, never the label (accidental twists). XR: r < 8 mm (3.5 mm pin + 4.5 mm reach) and from 5 mm below to 2 cm above the record surface. The rest of the label (to LABEL + 3 mm) does nothing. Desktop wheel: only within 6 mm of the spindle centre.
107. (27 Sep) Owner: spindle twist did too much -> geared down 75% (SPINDLE_GEAR 0.25: 10 deg twist = 12.5 ms, full turn = 0.45 s). Haptic tick still every 5 ms of shift (= every 4 deg of twist). Desktop wheel 1.25 ms a notch (Shift 0.25 ms). Record lift zone widened 5 mm into the label: now LABEL - 5 mm to LABEL + 2 cm (45-70 mm radius). The label inside 45 mm does nothing, and the spindle (r < 8 mm) still twists. Backup: backups/pre-107.
108. (27 Sep) Owner: spindle twist and platter nudge both 50% less, in both directions. Spindle: SPINDLE_GEAR 0.125 (10 deg = 6.25 ms, full turn = 0.225 s); desktop wheel 0.625 ms a notch (Shift 0.125 ms); haptic tick still every 5 ms of shift. Rim nudge: TOUCH_GRIP 0.12 -> 0.06. The platter's deviation from motor speed is grip x (hand - motor), so pushes and drags both have half the effect (a still finger now loses ~0.035 s per second instead of ~0.07). TOUCH_TAU unchanged (0.45 s). Backup: backups/pre-108.
109. (27 Sep) (a) Owner suspected the mixer beat LEDs run at the original BPM, not the pitched one (Close To You 96.01 / Dub Be Good To Me 95.93, ID3 = Rekordbox for both). Measured headless with the real files: the LEDs follow the pitched tempo. Pitch +8% / -4%: 107.9 and 89.9 steps/min vs 103.7 and 92.1 BPM; after LOCK 95.9 and 101.9 vs 97.89 BPM. Within +-6/min, one step of counting granularity in a 10 s window. Dub's 27 Rekordbox markers are all consistent with one 4/4 grid. What made them look wrong: (1) they showed the rendered playhead, ahead of the heard sound by the output latency; (2) they only redrew at the 15 fps screen tick, up to 67 ms late and uneven; (3) LOCK no longer aligns phase (#102), so the two decks' LEDs step at different moments until beat-matched by ear. Fix: ledBeat() uses playhead - (outputLatency + baseLatency) x rate, and the mixer screen redraws the instant either deck's beat changes. (b) Spindle twist and nudge halved again: SPINDLE_GEAR 0.0625 (10 deg = 3.1 ms, full turn 0.11 s), haptic tick every 2.5 ms, desktop wheel 0.31 ms a notch (Shift 0.06 ms); TOUCH_GRIP 0.03. Backup: backups/pre-109.
110. (27 Sep) Tap-to-set beat 1 (owner): a green BEAT 1 rubber button per channel, beside CUE towards the mixer's outer edge (x = +-0.108, z = 0.056). Controllers: trigger at the tip, or a fingertip poke, like the other mixer buttons. Press on a kick: that heard moment (playhead - output latency x rate) becomes beat 1. From then on the deck's LEDs count plain 4/4 at the track's BPM (ID3 first), carried along by pitch / platter speed as the record plays. Rekordbox grid markers are no longer used for that track. Remembered per track (localStorage vire.beat1.<trackId>); press again any time to re-set. Untapped tracks still use the Rekordbox grid. The button flashes on every beat 1 once a track is tapped. Desktop: click it; hover tip explains. Tested headless: tap set and saved; the 4/4 count cycles 1-2-3-4 from the tap (headless renders at 1.5 fps, so the per-frame LED check skips steps there; the maths sampled every 15 ms is clean). Backup: backups/pre-110.
111. (27 Sep) Owner: output distorting, as if both tracks add up with no limiter. There was a limiter (DynamicsCompressorNode, -1 dB, 20:1, knee 0, 2 ms / 100 ms), but it is not a brick-wall. Measured headless with Eve + Track II playing together: faders + master full -> peaks 1.10 (113 samples > 1.0 in 6 s); plus bass/trim boost -> 1.27 (7968 samples > 1.0), i.e. hard clipping at the output, and its 100 ms release pumped inside bass cycles. Fix: (a) new 'vire-limiter' AudioWorkletProcessor (in deck-worklet.js): 5 ms lookahead, hold, 250 ms release, stereo-linked, -1 dBFS ceiling plus a tanh soft knee above 0.94, so the output never passes 1.0. Gain reduction reported to engine.gainReduction. (b) 2.5 dB headroom on the music bus (masterBus 0.75), so two full tracks at the defaults peak about -5.5 dBFS and never touch the limiter. (c) Cue bus gets the same headroom and its own limiter (split-cue right ear could clip too). Same test after: max 0.889 in all three cases, 0 samples over 1.0 (limiter 0.2 dB at full faders/master, 9.5 dB with the extra boosts). Side effect: 2.5 dB quieter at the same knob settings; the master knob's range is unchanged (top = 0.9 overall now). Backup: backups/pre-111.
112. (27 Sep) Full backup: backups/ViRe_backup_2026-09-27_0155.zip (167 files, 32.1 MB, zip test OK). Same scope as #91/#95: web/ (without the Collection music junction), deploy_web/, CLAUDE.md, tools, renders, sources, Claude outputs, backups/pre-* folders (to pre-111). Left out: the Vinyl Reality APK zip and folder, _to_delete, older backup zips. Covers #96-#111.
113. (27 Sep) Mic echo (owner always uses wired headphones; records with the Quest's own recorder or streams with BeamXR). With headphones there is no speaker-to-mic loop, so the echo is the monitor path itself: voice -> browser input buffer -> mixer -> output buffer -> headphones arrives late against the voice heard through the head (worse in Recording-friendly mode, 'balanced' latency, and up to +50 ms with the PITCH knob off centre). Echo cancelling cannot remove it. Also, the Quest mic is exclusive to one app at a time (BeamXR help), and if the recorder's "Include mic audio" is on while the app plays the mic, the voice is recorded twice. Fix: start-screen setting Mic route (settings.micRoute). 'quest' (default) = the app never opens the mic; MIC on the mixer is talkover only (ducks the music by the LEVEL · DUCK knob's right half, LED lit steady); the voice goes into the recording/stream through Quest Camera > Settings > Include mic audio or BeamXR's mic, so nothing late reaches the headphones. 'app' = the old #60 chain (needed for voice PITCH); device list + Feedback guard only show for it; turn the recorder's/BeamXR's mic off with it; the MIC toast now shows the measured round trip (track latency + baseLatency + outputLatency). Switching route turns MIC off and releases the mic. Tested headless: quest route duck -24 dB with no mic opened, back to unity on MIC off; round-trip readout 50 ms on the container's default device (getUserMedia hangs in the container, so the app route's open was not re-run; that code is unchanged). deploy_web updated. Backup: backups/pre-113.
114. (27 Sep) Owner. (a) App renamed Cly3DJ: page title, start-screen heading (Cly3 + accent DJ), manifest name/short_name, the two install hints. Folder names, the /vire/ URL, localStorage 'vire.*' keys and the sw cache name stay, so saved layouts, settings and headset songs survive; an installed copy shows the new name after the browser picks up the manifest (reinstall if it doesn't). (b) Recording-friendly mode removed with its setting: always 90 Hz (updateTargetFrameRate), foveation 0.5, record textures 2048, real-time shadows per the Shadows setting, AudioContext latencyHint 'interactive'. Frame rate never touches audio or BPM: the decks run in the AudioWorklet on the audio hardware's sample clock; frames only draw the visuals and sample hand input (90 Hz = finer scratch/nudge steps). Risk to watch: the smaller 'interactive' audio buffers while the Quest also encodes a recording can crackle if the CPU is saturated; if that shows in recordings, raise only the audio buffer, not the frame rate. (c) Mic route: owner records with the Quest Camera's Include mic audio OFF, so 'app' (through the mixer) is now the default and listed first; Feedback guard default off (wired headphones always), getUserMedia asks for latency ideal 0 and echo cancelling only when the box is ticked. One-time migration (settings.mig114) moves saved settings to route 'app', guard off and drops 'perf'. The 'quest' talkover-only route is still in the list for BeamXR later. Tested headless (old and fresh settings): title/heading renamed, no Performance row, route 'app', mic rows shown, guard off, desktop start OK. Backup: backups/pre-114. deploy_web updated.
115. (27 Sep) Mixer readout glass (owner: too much reflection, wants it glossier, tilted less, the #89 bevel trick read as an artifact). (a) Screen + stand tilt 35 -> 25 deg back (TILT = -25 deg). (b) The whole #89 onBeforeCompile on the screen removed (4 mm parallax shift, 5 mm bevel bend with colour split, bevel glint); the parallax also clamped/smeared edge texels. Screen is a plain MeshBasic again. (c) Glass pane: roughness 0.03 -> 0 (sharp mirror reflection, tight highlight instead of a broad milky veil), specularIntensity 0.4 -> 0.2, specularF90 0.45 (limits the grazing-angle Fresnel wash). Still additive, black base, never dims the readout. Headless render from the DJ side: before = readout washed out by a large white blur; after = fully readable with a faint sheen along the top. Owner note from #114 test: the MIC "ms late" readout isn't visible in the headset (toasts are page overlays, not drawn in an immersive session); mic through the mixer works fine for him. Backup: backups/pre-115. deploy_web updated.
116. (27 Sep) Owner. SUPERSEDES the Rekordbox grid use in #69/#101/#103/#109/#110 and the "LOCK = tempo only" part of #102. (a) No Rekordbox beat grid anywhere: library.js no longer reads TEMPO, beatAt/applyGridFix/refineGrid and the worklet grids are gone. Beat 1 exists only if the DJ taps BEAT 1; an untapped deck shows no beat LEDs. BPM = ID3 TBPM, Rekordbox AverageBpm only when the file has none (#102). (b) Taps are saved in the browser's localStorage (per site address, per device: PC Chrome and Quest Browser each keep their own) under 'vire.tap.<artist>|<title>|<side>' = seconds into the file; old 'vire.beat1.<TrackID>' keys move over once. (c) Tap snap: decode-worker.js detectKicks() (120 Hz low band, 10 ms energy window stepped 2.5 ms, log rise against 10 % of the local level, peak within +-50 ms, then refineOnset() to the sample) runs after the PCM/grooves are posted, so it never delays loading; audio.js decode() returns kicks as a Promise. tapBeat1 snaps to the best attack within +-60 ms (own strength + recurrence 1-4 beats either side at the track BPM, small distance penalty), else keeps the raw tap; the toast says which. Tested on 8 of the owner's MP3s (read-only grant, test copies deleted): taps within 50 ms of a kick snap onto it 91-100 %; 60-200 ms per track on a desktop CPU. (d) LOCK = the 0.4 s meet-halfway glide, then (option 1, nudge only) the follower (not the last-touched pitch fader) is nudged to the NEAREST beat of the lead using both taps and BPMs, max 8 % speed change (LOCK_NUDGE_MAX), about one beat for small offsets. Bars are not aligned (beat jump = option 2, parked). Without taps on both decks LOCK is tempo only and says so. Headless: Eve (tap) + Track II (tap), +3 % / -2 %, LOCK -> 132.37 BPM both, offset -0.235 beat closed in 1.33 s, then 0.0000 beat. (e) Mixer readout: tap the big number or its label (desktop click, XR poke/controller tip) to cycle ORIG BPM (ID3 only, "no ID3 tag" otherwise) -> BPM (playing now) -> KEY (key it sounds in now: 12 x log2(speed x (1 + pitch)) semitones, e.g. "Gm" with "KEY · Abm -1.0 st"; key from ID3 TKEY, else Rekordbox Tonality; Camelot accepted). Per-deck mode saved in 'vire.bpmMode'. id3.js reads TKEY. Backup: backups/pre-116. deploy_web updated.
117. (27 Sep) Owner. SUPERSEDES #116 (c) (kick detection / tap snap removed: no audio analysis anywhere) and the BPM fallback in #102/#116. (a) BPM rule: the MP3's ID3 BPM (TBPM) always wins. Without one, a BPM tapped on BEAT 1 is used; with neither, the readout says "BPM · none, tap BEAT 1", the four beat dots stay grey and LOCK refuses (names the deck). Rekordbox AverageBpm is no longer used for playback/LOCK (it still fills the crate list's BPM column until the file's tag is read). (b) BEAT 1 = tap button (main.js tapBeat1/stepTaps): one tap sets beat 1 (heard moment, seconds into the song); further taps in the same run fit a least-squares line through the taps and update the tapped BPM live (readout "BPM · tapping (n)"). Taps are timed in song time, so pitch / platter speed don't change the result. A run ends after a pause of 1.75 beats (2.5 s before the 2nd tap), which suits slow songs (65 BPM = 0.92 s a beat); one missed beat is allowed; a tap under 60 % of a beat after the last is ignored; a tap far off the beat starts a new run. The run's first tap stays beat 1; a single tap after a pause re-sets beat 1 and keeps the saved BPM. (c) Long press 0.8 s clears beat 1 + tapped BPM for that track; the BEAT 1 LED blinks 4 times. Mouse: held button. XR fingertip / controller tip: must keep resting on the button (keep-alive every frame, xr.js holdBeat1/releaseBeat1); a tracking gap over 0.15 s cancels the hold, so a lost hand never clears. (d) Storage: per track key '<artist>|<title>|<side>' -> { beat1, bpm, taps, updated }. Always in localStorage 'vire.taps' (the #116 'vire.tap.*' and #110 'vire.beat1.<TrackID>' keys move over), and auto-saved 0.4 s after a change to the sidecar web/cly3dj_taps.json through a new server.js endpoint GET/POST /vire/api/taps (tapsFile on the /vire mount; per-key merge, newer 'updated' wins, deletions carried, atomic write, previous file kept as .bak; backup server.js.bak-before-taps). Needs a server restart to take effect. On load the app merges the sidecar with local data (newest wins, local-only entries are sent up). Without the endpoint (static host) it reads a static cly3dj_taps.json if present and keeps saves on the device; failed saves retry every 30 s. The readout shows "taps saved" / "saving…" / "saved on this device" / "not saved: retrying". ORIG mode shows "· tap x" next to the ID3 BPM. (e) Tested headless with deterministic song/wall clocks (the headless renderer's 1.5 fps makes real-time taps meaningless): 8 taps at 100 BPM +-15 ms jitter -> 99.79; at +8 % pitch with one skipped beat -> 99.93; 65 BPM -> 64.84; ID3 track tapped at 125 keeps 124 for playback; sidecar survives a reload with localStorage cleared; long press clears in the sidecar too; lost-tracking hold does not clear. Backup: backups/pre-117. deploy_web updated.
118. (27 Sep) Owner: the crate list always shows the Rekordbox XML BPM (AverageBpm), also after a record's ID3 tag has been read or it goes back to the crate; crate BPM search uses the same value. useID3Bpm no longer overwrites record.bpm; only the decks use the ID3 BPM (track.bpm, bpmSrc 'id3'). Later option: read BPM/key from each MP3's tag for the crate. deploy_web updated.
119. (27 Sep) Owner: not every MP3 has an ID3 BPM, but every track in the XML has AverageBpm. SUPERSEDES the BPM rule in #117 (a). BPM priority for decks, LEDs and LOCK: ID3 TBPM > Rekordbox XML AverageBpm ("RB", used as ground truth, a single number) > tapped BPM (only if both are missing). Rekordbox beat grids are still never used for timing; beat 1 only ever comes from the BEAT 1 tap. Readout: BPM mode shows "BPM · RB" for XML-sourced tempo; ORIG mode shows "RB:BPM" with the XML value when the file has no ID3 BPM, or "ORIG BPM · ID3 · RB:x" when it has both. main.js fileBpmSrc / rbBpmOf / bpmOf. Tested headless: ID3 track 124 + no-ID3 track (XML 99) -> LOCK 111.5 both. Backup: backups/pre-119. deploy_web updated.
120. (27 Sep) Turntable physics (owner: spec-accurate, Classic MK2 default, pitch through the motor, real servo on the rim, no real deck to measure). SUPERSEDES the platter/nudge model of #45/#85/#90/#108/#109 (kept as 'Before physics' in settings). (a) deck-worklet.js: each deck = platter body + record body (record + 25 g slipmat), stepped every audio sample. Playback rate = the RECORD's speed; the platter keeps turning under a held record. Motor = MK2-style quartz-PLL servo: P on speed (kp 0.5 N m per rad/s) + I on angle (ki 3.5) with the angle term clamped to half an FG pulse (pi/91) and anti-windup; torque limit 0.147 N m (1.5 kg cm) tapering 15 % toward 45 rpm; bi-directional (STOP = the motor's own reverse torque, released at 0.5 % speed so a stopped platter spins freely by hand). Platter inertia 0.024 kg m2 (set so start-up matches the 0.7 s spec); bearing 0.0022 N m (KAB); cogging 0.0028 N m x sin(48 theta) (Classic only); drag 0.0077 N m per rad/s chosen to keep the ~7 s power-off coast of #48 (not measured). Stylus drag 0.0012 N m on the record. Slipmat = stick-slip Coulomb friction, mean radius 2/3 R: slick (felt on slipsheet) muS 0.24 / muK 0.18, felt 0.45 / 0.35, rubber 1.5 / 1.2; a holding hand adds 2 N down. Hand = 60 Hz critically damped spring from the hand ANGLE (main.js now sends the unwrapped hand angle, dead-reckoned up to 30 ms between frames), grip effectively unlimited. Rim finger = friction mu 0.6 x force: controllers = analogue trigger 0.35-2 N, fingertip = depth inside the platter edge 0.35-2 N (0-12 mm), pinch 0.6 N. LOCK nudge now scales the motor reference. PLL phase memory option (off: the angle term returns to its quiet value once hands are off and the record grips again). Modern profile (MK7-style): 0.18 N m, I 0.0245, no cogging, START ramped over 0.7 s, stiffer servo (gains are estimates). (b) Measured in Node on the real worklet (Classic, 180 g, slick): start 0.72 s to 99 % (0.79 s with a record), 0.5 % overshoot; STOP 0.63 s; power-off coast 7.3 s to 1 %; still finger on the rim 0.35 N: dips to 0.986, back to 1.000, costs 2.9 ms; 0.8 N: 0.969, recovers, 6.5 ms; 1.5 N: stalls to ~0.30 while held; holding the record still: record 0, platter 1.000 (min 0.972); release: record at 99 % in 0.23 s; backspin -2.5x runs forward again after 0.54 s; pitch 0 -> +8 % settles in 0.11 s; 33 -> 45 in 0.43 s; LOCK nudge +5 % x 0.5 s = +25.00 ms exactly; 3 Hz +-90 deg scratch tracks the hand within 3 deg; cogging: slow hand spin released rocks back and rests on a detent. Headless browser (desktop start): no page errors, worklet reports model/platter angle, start 0.73 s. (c) main.js: platter and record drawn from separate angles (platter from the worklet's platter angle, record from the playhead / record angle); strobe dots use the platter's speed; 'record' message on place / pick-up (a record dropped on a spinning platter lands still and the mat pulls it up to speed); flip needs the platter stopped too; settings Turntable motor / Record weight / Slipmat / PLL phase memory on the start screen (vire.settings deckModel, recWeight, slipmat, pll). xr.js: rim force, controller haptics (cogging tick per 1/48 turn when the Classic motor isn't driving; soft rumble with mat slip and motor strain). No server change, no restart needed: reload the page. Backup: backups/pre-physics. deploy_web updated.
121. (27 Sep) Owner shared Gemini's figures: START 0.7 s, STOP ~0.7-0.8 s, power-off coast several s to 10+ s. Checked the MK2 service manual: it gives 0.7 s build-up, 1.5 kg cm, 2 kg platter, 'electronic brake', but NO stop time (Gemini's brake figure comes from reviews of later 1200s). Our #120 STOP was 0.63 s, so the brake now uses 0.8 of the motor's torque limit (BRAKE_GAIN): Classic STOP 0.71 s to 1 % without a record, 0.77 s with a 180 g record, standstill ~0.9 s (Modern 0.59 / 0.64 s). Coast unchanged (7.3 s to 1 %, standstill ~9.4 s), inside Gemini's range. Also added static friction at rest: an untouched, unpowered platter below 0.004 rad/s stops instead of rocking in a cogging detent for seconds (it still rocks into the nearest detent when the detent pull beats the bearing, per KAB). Start, rim, hold, release, backspin, LOCK (+25.00 ms) results unchanged. deck-worklet.js only; reload the page, no server restart. deploy_web updated.

124. (27 Sep) First Blender asset via the Blender Lab MCP (live in the owner's Blender 5.1.2, OptiX on the RTX 4050): the milk crate. blender/milk_crate/milk_crate.blend holds the source. Model: same size, origin and planes as MILK (345 x 345 x 280 mm, walls 12 mm, floor top 8 mm) so the crate physics is unchanged; rounded-corner shell (15 mm vertical corner radius) with 7 x 3 openings per wall, 110 x 27 mm stadium hand holes in the top band of the x walls, grid floor with 7 x 7 holes; booleans (exact), triangulated: 3,024 triangles, flat normals. UVs: smart project + concave pack, ~37 % coverage (the lattice holes waste the rest), ~1.4 px/mm at 2K. Bakes (Cycles GPU; first bake ~5 min for OptiX kernel compile, then 2-6 s each): tangent-space NORMAL from a Bevel node (2.2 mm rounded moulded edges) + fine grain bump; AO (6 cm reach, 128 samples); base colour (green HDPE 0a421e..0e5226 mottling, paler scuffs only at the rim top and bottom edge); roughness 0.26-0.34 (scuffs 0.5); packed ORM (R AO, G rough, B metal 0). Normal rebaked after triangulation so its tangents match the exported TANGENT attribute. Export: web/models/milk_crate.glb (4.0 MB): normal 2K PNG, base colour + ORM 1K PNG, KHR_materials_clearcoat 0.6 / 0.15 (keeps the #68 gloss). App: models.js loadMilkCrateBaked() + upgradeMilkCrate(); main.js loads it at start and swaps the body of every existing crate (extra crates made later use it directly); the procedural crate stays as the fallback if the GLB fails. Material FrontSide (closed shell). Checked in the owner's Chrome (built-in browser, his GPU): baked material with map/normal/ao/roughness/metalness, 1 draw call, 3,024 triangles for the crate. GPU memory ~30 MB for its three textures (normal 2K is most of it; 1K would be ~11 MB total). Reload the page, no server restart. Backup: backups/pre-milkglb. deploy_web updated (GLB included).

Non-metal UV islands packed at half scale so metal gets ~0.84 px/mm. Per-object bakes composited with rasterised UV masks + 8 px dilation (Blender's own margin would overwrite the other objects' islands). 132. (28 Sep) KTX2 / Basis compressed textures. Vendored from it into web/vendor/three: loaders/KTX2Loader.js, utils/WorkerPool.js, libs/ktx-parse.module.js, libs/zstddec.module.js, libs/basis/basis_transcoder.js + .wasm (same r169). Conversion runs in Blender (text block ktx_lib in blender/record_crate/record_crate.blend: to_ktx2(src_glb, dst_glb)): each embedded image -> toktx --t2 --encode uastc --uastc_quality 2 --zcmp 19 --genmipmap (--assign_oetf srgb for base colour/emissive, linear otherwise), GLB rewritten with KHR_texture_basisu (required). Outputs web/models/milk_crate_ktx.glb (528 KB) and record_crate_ktx.glb (851 KB); the PNG GLBs stay as fallback. App: models.js initKTX2(renderer) (called once from main.js) + loadBaked(url) tries *_ktx.glb first, falls back to the PNG GLB. sw.js LATE list adds the transcoder js/wasm for offline start. Measured in the owner's Chrome: textures arrive compressed (BC7 on his RTX 4050; Quest 3 will get ASTC 4x4), per crate 6.7 -> 1.7 MB of GPU memory (1K normal 1.33 MB + 512 ORM 0.33 MB with mips), look unchanged. The case is resizable, so it is built from stretchable pieces, not a fixed model: CaseAngle (real aluminium angle: two 22 mm flanges, 3 mm thick, 3 mm rounded outer corner; unit length along x), CaseRail (tongue-and-groove lid seam, 30 x 5 mm), CaseCorner (ball corner with three quarter-disc wings), CaseLatch, CaseHandle. One trim-sheet material for all of them: vertex colours for base colours; 1024 x 512 normal + occlusion/roughness/metal (row 0 = the angle strip, brushed, a rivet every 70 mm on each flange; row 1 = the rail; row 2 = flat cells for chrome / dark / black plastic / bronze). Laminate: CaseLamTile's material, dark grey 3b3d42 like the record crate (#128, reads on AR eyewear), tileable 512 pebble normal map, world-scale UVs (one tile = 0.16 m) so the grain keeps its size at any case size. flightcase.js buildKit(): places and stretches the pieces (strip UVs tile every 0.14 m, rivets centred on each edge), angles stop 22 mm short of each corner under the corner wings (no overlaps), bottom balls flattened (y 0.45) since the case stands on them, lid-seam rail cut under each latch dish; mirrored placements flip winding and tangent sign. Resize zones are invisible boxes on the bottom corners and bottom edges (the old bottom extrusions carried the resize tag). main.js loads the kit after creating the case and rebuilds it; without the kit the procedural case is built as before. Measured: 15 -> 2 draw calls, 20.1k -> 3.5k triangles (the 15.4k instanced rivets are now in the normal map), rebuild on resize ~2 ms. Backups: backups/pre-casekit.

137. (28 Sep) Record texture memory (owner chose option 1). Outside the label everything on a record side depends only on the radius, so textures.js drawRecordSide now fills a 1024 x 1 RGBA ring strip (RGB = vinyl colour sRGB, A = the roughness byte) with exactly the old rules: rim, lead-in, one ring per step OUT..IN from the averaged loudness envelope (L = 5 + 30 e^0.8, hsl 225/6%), run-out, the 2 px run-out line, roughness values of #92/#93/#99, label 200. 1024 texels over R = the old 2048 canvas's density. The label is its own canvas (512; 128 for far/loose records) covering LABEL_BUMP_R on uv1 (same mapping as the relief map; labelBumpMap takes label-only canvases via canvas.vireLabelR). recordMaterial() (MeshPhysical, same #58/#93 settings): the shader takes colour and roughness from the strip by distance from the disc centre, blends to the label at REC.LABEL (anti-aliased with fwidth), and draws hot cue marks (5 px, 0.18 rad at 12 o'clock of the old canvas) from uniforms (max 16). setRecordSide() swaps a side in and disposes the old maps. The crate's riding disc uses the same material with no label map (its label is the separate circles). Per record side ~1.4 MB (was ~24 MB): a record ~2.8 MB instead of ~47 MB; the crate disc ~0 instead of ~22 MB. LOD switches now only redraw the label (3-6 ms; the two 2048 canvases are gone). Checked in the owner's Chrome: top-down render, bands follow the envelope (strip lightness vs raw envelope r = 0.68 over 564 rings, lead-in to run-out), cue marks, label, gloss unchanged. Note: building the label relief (labelBumpMap, #100) still takes ~0.24 s on first approach; unchanged here. Backups: backups/pre-recstrip. Project doc claude/groove-rendering-rules.md updated.

142. (28 Sep) Owner: a collider on the underside of the record crate and the milk crates so they stack instead of cutting through. main.js: stackTops(key) = everything under the crate's footprint (oriented rectangles in rig space, SAT overlap, own footprint inset 1 cm so crates can stand snugly side by side): flight cases (top = y + H), the other crates (CRATE / MILK sizes) and the gear (decks, mixer: own-frame bounding box of their visible meshes, measured once, cached in userData.__lbox). clampStack(key): while a crate is carried (headset hand move, desktop drag, desktop Shift+wheel height) its bottom never goes below the highest top under it, so moving it over something lifts it onto it. settleStack(key): on release it drops onto the top just under it if within 6 cm (else stays put: no gravity, #42). A tossed milk crate lands on the top under it instead of always on the floor (not when thrown away). Stacked crates don't ride along when the lower one is moved (only flight cases carry riders).
