The Quest and phone must both run the same channel's code (the old handshake and the new one don't understand each other).
363. (15 Oct) Owner batch. (a) Crate: the record riding out of the selected sleeve showed in front of it (whole disc and label over the cover). Cause: the crate disc was drawn a real 1.9 mm thick with labels 1.15 mm off its centre, but the jacket is a 1.4 mm card with its cover 0.9 mm off the centre, so the disc poked through both faces. crateDisc.userData.size now draws it 0.6 mm thick (labels at 0.4 mm), inside the jacket: the jacket's front now hides the part inside the sleeve. (b) It pops out less: 0.85 x 0.85 of before (owner: 15 % less, then a further 15 %): a 12" record stands ~7.5 cm above its jacket's top (was ~10.4 cm), computed per record size from the jacket top. (c) A record taken into the hand showed side B with the right hand: the hold put the disc's up face (side A) along the hand's +X, which points right for both hands. Now, when a hand takes a record (any new attach: crate, deck, sleeve), it picks +X or -X, whichever points toward the eyes (qHoldXR / qHoldXR2, held.qHold). Checked with pretend left and right hands: A faces the eyes for both. (d) Tablet BACK on every page (owner): navTrack() in drawMixScreen records each page change (toolsPage, VISUALS tab, LOOK sub-page, scroller-from-VISUALS) in a 40-deep history; '◀ BACK' at the top right goes to the previous page (dim when there is none). The page's own top-right button moves left of it: main page VISUALS; VISUALS pages DJ MIXER (landscape tabs narrowed to fit; portrait has a new header row VISUALS title + DJ MIXER + BACK, tabs one row lower, content from y 192); TOOLS pages: DJ MIXER on TOOLS home, the parent as '▲ TOOLS' / '▲ STREAM' / '▲ VJ TOOLS' etc. elsewhere (the old '◀ BACK' that went to the parent). LED LIST's own bottom back button now reads '◀ VIDEO' / '◀ IMAGES'. Portrait CAM FIT title shortened. Also fixed: portrait TOOLS home note was drawn under the CHALKBOARD button (#342). (e) VJ TOOLS: AVATAR: ON / OFF (the robot DJ for AVACAM / SHOWTIME!, same switch as the AVACAM tab's AVATAR), beside LED WALL and NEON. Test hook vire._nav. Checked in the cloud (headless Chromium, SwiftShader): crate renders front / side, 50 tablet pages in both orientations with no label cut, BACK walk VISUALS > TOOLS pages > main in order.
364. (15 Oct) Owner: with no record on the platter, holding the slipmat's edge didn't stop it; on a real SL-1200 the mat is its own piece and slips over the platter with friction. Before, with no record the worklet had no mat body at all (record angle = platter angle) and xr.js ignored a hand on the bare mat. Now: deck-worklet.js, no record + a hand on the mat = the record-body slip model with the mat alone (25 g felt, R 15 cm: IR_MAT, its own hand spring, normal force = mat weight + the 2 N hand press, no stylus drag); let go = the mat grips again. xr.js: controller trigger anywhere on the bare mat holds it (the record-edge rule of #273), bare hands: a pinch holds it (never lifts it: pinchHold.mat) or a fingertip resting on it (r > 3 cm); main.js: the mat pivot (#275) slips by the worklet's record angle with no record too. Measured on the real worklet in Node (Classic motor, slick slipsheet): mat held still, friction 0.041 N m, the platter dips to 98.4 % for a moment and the quartz servo brings it back to 100 %; let go: the mat back at speed at once; with a record: unchanged. Not tried in the headset.
365. (15 Oct) Owner. (a) STREAM page: the CHAT button sat over the #363 '▲ TOOLS' button. head() takes an optional extra button, drawn left of the parent button in the header row (landscape: title in the rest; portrait: it takes the title's place). (b) Crate: scrolling the first ~10 records, the new selection's record showed in front of the sleeves for a moment, then sank in. Cause: on a new selection the riding disc was reset low but kept the OLD sleeve's z and glided across (with fewer than ~9 records before it the window doesn't scroll, so the selected sleeve changes place; further on it stays put, which is why only the first ten showed it). Now it starts inside its own sleeve (position z set too). (c) Preview orientation check on the tablet's pop-up screen: Quest side (createImageBitmap flipY + texture flipY false, the lid picture turned -/+90 deg for a tablet on its side, image right = world right) and the phone's AR preview turn (#205) are correct by the maths. Gap found in fixed camera mode: the phone sent its screen canvas unturned, so a phone on its side with auto-rotate off (portrait page) showed sideways. fixedPreview now turns it upright by the calibrated camera's roll, same rule / dead zone as AR. Checked in the cloud: 50 tablet pages, no label cut, STREAM header in both orientations. Not checked on the M33 / Quest: the fixed-mode turn, the crate scrolling feel. Note: LIVE / PHONE CAM in fixed mode still send the screen unturned (not asked).
366. (15 Oct) Release: #363 to #365 copied from cly3dj-dev to cly3dj (js/deck-worklet.js, main.js, spectator-client.js, xr.js): crate record rides inside its sleeve and pops out less (0.85 x 0.85), side A toward the eyes in the hand, tablet BACK on every page + AVATAR on VJ TOOLS, the bare slipmat can be held (slips over the platter with felt friction), STREAM header CHAT clear of TOOLS / BACK, a newly selected crate record starts inside its own sleeve, fixed-camera preview turned upright. The #365 crate fix was confirmed in the cloud against the old code: a new selection used to start 110 / 26 / 11 mm off its sleeve (2nd / 3rd / 4th record) and ~8 mm further on; now 0 mm. cly3dj matches cly3dj-dev again (apart from CLAUDE.md).
367. (15 Oct) Owner: VISUALS > PANO > SKY OFF didn't turn the panorama off (passthrough should show again). Two causes: (1) SKY OFF was drawn active with no thumbnail picked, but vpAct checked for a picked thumbnail first and returned with a desktop-only toast, so nothing happened in the headset; (2) it only set settings.sky = 'off', while in passthrough the pano shows through the DOME (#346), which stayed on. Now SKY OFF runs before that check and turns the sky setting and the DOME off (applySky hides the grounded sky, pauses a video pano, brings back the room in VR / passthrough in AR). The environment reflections keep the picked image (Environment light setting, unchanged). Checked in the cloud: sky on + dome on, nothing picked -> SKY OFF -> sky off, dome off, skybox hidden. DOME / SKY bring it back as before.
368. (15 Oct) Owner: chalkboard slate black, and the chalks / duster must never grow past real-life size. chat.js: board base #17191b (was greenish #1d2a24), smudges and old wipe marks neutral grey. The ledge (shelf, lip, three chalks, duster) now sits in its own frame at the board's bottom edge, counter-scaled by 1 / board size (_ledgeFit on scaleTo, reset and the saved size at load); only the shelf and lip stretch to the board's width. Ledge touches are tested in real metres. Checked in the cloud: at 1x and 4x the chalk is 5.5 cm long and the duster 8 cm; touching the duster at its real place picks it.
369. (15 Oct) Release: #367 (SKY OFF turns the panorama and DOME off) and #368 (slate black chalkboard, real-size chalks / duster) copied from cly3dj-dev to cly3dj (js/main.js, js/chat.js). cly3dj matches cly3dj-dev again (apart from CLAUDE.md). Owner declined stream filters (VHS etc.); a laser prop (beams, fans, scanner text / emoji, depth-map crowd contours) is under discussion, nothing built.
370. (16 Oct) Owner batch. (a) CHALKBOARD tray follows the board's lowest edge (chat.js): turn the board on its side, either way, or upside down and the shelf, lip, three chalks and duster move to whichever edge is lowest in the room, turned so they stay upright; the side edges are board-height long (portrait side), the shelf and lip run that edge's full length. Still counter-scaled (#368) so chalks stay 5.5 cm and the duster 8 cm at any board size. Ledge touches are now measured in the ledge's own frame (real metres), so picking a chalk / the duster works on any edge. A 0.12 margin stops it flipping near 45 deg; a board lying flat keeps its edge. Checked in Node with real three.js: upright, both sideways, upside down, 20 and 60 deg tilts, at 1x and 4x: shelf always at the lowest edge, ledge-up always world up, ledge world scale 1.000. Not checked on the Quest. (b) CARD plate carries a Twitch QR (stream.js): top right, white plate with quiet zone, 7 px modules (about 260 px), 'FOLLOW ON TWITCH' and twitch.tv/dirrogate under it. The code says https://www.twitch.tv/dirrogate (twitch.com only redirects there). Made offline (Python qrcode, version 3, level M, 29 x 29) and kept as rows of 0 / 1 in stream.js (QR_ROWS), drawn once into its own canvas; to change the address, remake the rows. Checked: the real drawCard rendered, decoded by OpenCV, also after halving to 640 x 360 and JPEG quality 40. (c) SCROLLER showed only as a narrow, tall strip in the middle of a landscape LED wall (tools.js, ledwall.js). Cause: the curved wall (#358) bends its edges toward the viewer and the scroller was a flat plane, so beyond a few centimetres of the centre it sank inside the screen; measured 14 / 10 / 8 % of the width visible at 45 / 90 / 150 deg. Now on a curved wall the band is bent onto the same circle (same formula as ledwall.js), 3 mm in front of the screen at every point (checked at 45, 90, 150 deg); flat and portrait walls unchanged (a portrait wall stays flat). ledwall.js gets userData.curveRad() (the exact curve, so the band matches the frame). The phone's wall uses the same code. No new files, so sw.js is unchanged. Not checked on the Quest.
371. (16 Oct) Decisions filed, laser and crowd (owner, nothing built yet). Laser: RGB colours; one positionable GoPro-style laser head; haze on; auto helpers on; the owner moderates himself, and is considering a separate Cly3DVJ page (every visual side by side, to load on a phone / iPad / Android tablet beside a dedicated VJ). Twitch shouts: a viewer sends !shout (144 characters) or a Channel Points reward with text; messages wait in a queue on a SHOUTS tablet page with LED, LASER, BOTH, REJECT, plus a word filter, one per viewer per few minutes, emoji kept, links stripped, optional AUTO play; club-goers can scan a QR to a small send page that reaches the headset through the ntfy.sh relay; Twitch Bits to pay for a shout later. Crowd contours from the headset's depth map (not the phone's, parked): best seen in HEADSET CAM; AVACAM shows it as a point-cloud 'depth crowd'; a Quest depth test comes first. Club screens: a CLUB SCREEN page (screen.html) fed from the Quest over WebRTC on the club Wi-Fi (about 0.1 to 0.2 s, in sync with the PA, unlike Twitch at 3 to 10 s); the M33 can't be an HDMI bridge; Meta Cast to a Chromecast works today but shows first person only. Order: depth test, laser prop, SHOUTS, CLUB SCREEN, crowd contours. Current state: QR on the CARD plate is in (#370); SHOUTS is not built.
372. (16 Oct) CONFIRMED REBUILD FOR SHOWTIME! (owner's redesign; discussed, NOTHING BUILT YET; build only on the owner's go). WHY: today SHOWTIME! is three fixed layers (visuals backdrop, robot + gear, flat overlays) and PHONE CAM / HEADSET CAM / AVACAM are separate stream sources with their own pipelines. The owner wants one modular live shot. The same composed frame must go out to Twitch AND to local club screens (a separate screen.html page on the club network, shown on any TV, e.g. by Meta Cast; see #371). CONFIRMED BY THE OWNER: (1) SHOWTIME! = a camera pick plus object toggles. (2) Camera pick: GOPRO, FRONT, FADERS, HEADSET CAM, and PHONE CAM folded in (owner: 'it needs to be folded in'); zoom WIDE / NORMAL / TIGHT and CAM FIT stay with their cameras. HEADSET CAM is a camera choice, not a toggle (owner: 'yes, it makes sense'). (3) Object toggles: Robot, Gear, GoPro (the prop), Dome, LED wall, Chalkboard, Scroller, Chat panel, Neon. (4) Under HEADSET CAM the Robot and the flat LED wall picture become redundant and turn off; the owner's other choices are kept and come back when leaving HEADSET CAM. (5) The GoPro is in twice: as the camera (already an AVACAM angle, goproToDjcam) and as a prop that other cameras can film, with its tally lamp while on air. It is hidden only from its own camera (today djPrep and showPrep hide it from every shot); its toggle is greyed out when it is the camera. (6) The Scroller must show ON the LED wall (owner: it never shows on the wall, only flat over everything). Cause found: showPrep hides scroller.mesh and the flat scroller layer is always drawn on top. Intent: on the 3D wall when the wall is in shot, flat overlay when it is not, never both. (Wall curve fix is #370c.) PROPOSED BY CLAUDE, NOT YET ANSWERED: backdrop as one three-way choice NONE / VISUALS / DOME (no stacking; the 3D LED wall stays a separate object); presets FULL, LEAN, FIRST PERSON; a render-time readout (DjCam.ms) beside the toggles; NOW PLAYING and LOGO stay as small flat extras; old vire.showOpts toggles migrate to the new ones; Neon needs a note or a flip on FRONT / FADERS / low because DJ_HIDE_NEON hides it there (its back faces the camera). OPEN QUESTION: under HEADSET CAM, is the 3D LED wall also forced off, or only the flat backdrop (Claude's reading)? Also: how screen.html receives the frame (the earlier plan opened it on an HDMI device and sent video over WebRTC; the owner now says it is seen via Meta Cast), to settle before the club screen is built. FOUND IN THE CODE: main.js djPrep() hides ledwall in every AVACAM render, so AVACAM, and the stream's AVACAM source, never show the LED wall (it is hidden to avoid a feedback loop, which only happens when the wall itself shows AVACAM, ledMode 'cam'); showPrep() hides it in the SHOWTIME! robot layer too (the wall's content comes in as the flat full-frame backdrop instead). PLANNED FIX: hide the wall only when ledMode === 'cam'; the FRONT camera sits on the wall's side looking back at the DJ, so a wall seen in shot must be placed behind the DJ facing the camera. COST NOTES (estimates from how things are built, not measured on the Quest): scroller, chat panel negligible; robot small (7.5k triangles); LED wall quad small; chalkboard small (texture upload only while writing); gear moderate; DOME light with a still pano, moderate with a video pano; NEON heavy (its point light costs every pixel, #334). The per-frame read-back to the encoder stays the same whatever is toggled, so toggles save GPU render time, not CPU. BUILD ORDER (when approved): (a) the AVACAM wall fix; (b) a per-object hide list replacing the fixed hides in showPrep / djPrep; (c) the camera pick, GoPro prop rule, and PHONE CAM folded in (read pcamPrep and the pcam code in stream.js first); (d) the tablet page: toggles, presets, cost readout; (e) saved-settings migration; then (f) one composed frame feeding both Twitch and the club screen page. Not checked: HEADSET CAM's render code (_mrFrame) in full; nothing here has been tested on the Quest.
373. (16 Oct) #372 REBUILD FOR SHOWTIME! STATUS & ANSWERS (owner confirmed; steps a, b, c, d, e built; step f and #371 parked). OWNER DECISIONS: (1) Under HEADSET CAM, 3D LED wall and robot are forced off; other toggles stay intact and return when leaving. (2) screen.html workflow: Fire TV stick connected to a TV in the club opens Cly3DJ TV (screen.html), enters a code and connects via WebRTC to display whatever is broadcasting to Twitch at lower latency. (3) All 5 proposals agreed (NONE / VISUALS / DOME backdrop, presets, render-time readout, settings migration, Neon note). (4) Neon visibility: if toggled on, seen in FRONT cam, GOPRO and HEADSET (the old FRONT / FADERS / low suppression removed). (5) Camera pick row: GOPRO / FRONT / FADERS / HEADSET CAM / PHONE CAM as one pick. PHONE CAM in this context = calibrated front or back camera from the phone streamed to Quest 3; Quest 3 overlays the decks and other objects as selected on the OBJECTS page. (6) Wait on step (f) (one composed frame / screen.html) and wait on #371 items (depth test, laser prop, SHOUTS, crowd contours). BUILT: (a) main.js djPrep() hides ledwall only when ledMode === 'cam' (feedback loop prevention). GoPro prop hidden only from its own camera (gopro.on). (b) Per-object hide list: showPrep() unified into propHides(hide, O, ownCam) driven by streamer.showOpt (wall, backdrop, neon, gopro, chat panel / chalkboard, gear, scroller). Neon follows toggle everywhere (DJ_HIDE_NEON suppression bypassed when streamer active). Scroller rule: 3D LED wall when facing camera and inside view frustum (_wallShot via wallInShot), flat overlay when not, never both. GEAR FIX: the turntable shells are 2-instance InstancedMeshes (deckInst, #154) added directly to scene (not children of deckGroups), so hiding deckGroups left the bodies visible; propHides() now also hides each deckInst pair (p.im) and held record when GEAR is off. (c) Camera pick row & folding in: SHOWTIME! OBJECTS page (`showobj`) has CAMERA pick row: GOPRO, FRONT, FADERS, HEADSET CAM, PHONE CAM (camPick). Selecting GOPRO / FRONT / FADERS sets djSet.preset and ensures stream source is 'show'. Selecting HEADSET CAM or PHONE CAM sets stream source directly. Stream page sources row simplified (5 buttons: CARD, AVACAM, SHOWTIME!, VJ SETUP, PHONE), where SHOWTIME! stays highlighted when HEADSET CAM or PHONE CAM is active. HEADSET CAM mrPrep() forces off ledwall and avatar, while applying propHides for remaining objects. PHONE CAM pcamPrep() uses propHides to overlay gear and selected objects over the incoming phone video. (d) Tablet: Stream page has BACKDROP cycle button (NONE / VISUALS / DOME) and OBJECTS ▸ navigation button. New SHOWTIME! OBJECTS page (`showobj`) with CAMERA row, OBJECT toggles (wall, gopro, neon, panel, chalk, gear, robot), PRESETS (full, lean, first), and live render-time readout (djcam.ms). (e) Migration: stream.js migrateShowOpts converts old visuals boolean to backdrop ('visuals' / 'none') and defaults new toggles. (f) Stream note: GO LIVE button is dimmed/disabled when !S.hasKey() && !S.live; requires a Twitch Stream Key saved in localStorage ('vire.twitchKey'), entered via start screen #sTwitchKey or console. NOT BUILT (PARKED / WAITING): Step (f) composed frame streaming to club screen (screen.html WebRTC client + room code); #371 items (depth test, laser prop, SHOUTS queue/ntfy relay, crowd contours). Files touched: web/js/stream.js, web/js/main.js (no new files, sw.js unchanged). node --check clean on both.
374. (16 Oct) PHONE CAM FOLDED INTO SHOWTIME! (owner: drop the PHONE and PHONE CAM SETUP buttons from STREAM, PHONE CAM is a camera source on SHOWTIME! that opens its settings page with a small preview; drop the 3D LED wall in the phone shot; handle props that would cut the DJ's head; Option A). WHY the wall and props cut the DJ: the phone shot is flat video, and PCAM_FS composites backdrop, then the keyed DJ, then the 3D layer on top (mix(c, gc, a)), with no depth for the DJ; so any 3D object, even one standing behind him, is painted over his head. The dome and the VISUALS backdrop are never part of the 3D layer (they go in the background texture under the cutout, and pcamPrep hides the sky), so they cannot cover him. BUILT (main.js, stream.js; no new files, sw.js unchanged): (1) STREAM page: PHONE and PHONE CAM SETUP buttons removed (sources now CARD, AVACAM, SHOWTIME!, VJ SETUP; PORTALS row two buttons); ROBOT on STREAM is greyed unless the DJ camera is the source. A saved source 'phone' (no button any more) becomes 'show'. (2) OBJECTS page: tapping PHONE CAM selects it and opens the PHONE CAM page (back goes to OBJECTS); if the phone is not there the button stays tappable and a toast says why (pcamReady). With PHONE CAM selected the page shows a PHONE CAM block: status, GREEN SCREEN, LATENCY MATCH, LIGHT MATCH, SETUP. LED WALL and ROBOT are greyed under PHONE CAM like HEADSET (the toggles keep their saved values). (3) PHONE CAM page: BG button replaced by BACK (the shared SHOWTIME! backdrop NONE / VISUALS / DOME, only seen with GREEN SCREEN); status text shared with the OBJECTS block (pcamStatus); a small preview of the stream picture on the right, redrawn about 5 times a second (page timer 200 ms); while the PHONE CAM or CROP page is open the shot runs off air (rehearsal) when it is not already live or on PREVIEW, and stops on leaving. (4) stream.js: PCAM_DEF.bg removed; the keyed shot's background follows showOpt.backdrop (dome from the phone's view, VISUALS frame, or the dark gradient for NONE); migration: a saved PHONE CAM BG=DOME carries over once as backdrop=dome only if no SHOWTIME! settings were saved, then the old key is deleted. _wallShot is always false in the phone shot (scroller is the flat band). (5) main.js pcamPrep(cam): ledwall always hidden in this shot; Option A: a prop (neon, chat panel / chalkboard, GoPro prop) is left out when it is farther from the phone than the DJ's head (headset position), measured from its bounding-box centre along the phone camera's axis, 15 cm to leave out, 5 cm to bring back (no flicker); only on the headset (off it nothing is left out). Gear is not subject to the rule (decks and mixer stand in front of the DJ). CHECKED: node --check and an eslint no-undef comparison (no new undefined names, both files); Node tests with real three.js and the real behindDj text from main.js (11 cases: behind / in front / beside, hysteresis, group, hidden object, off the headset, phone moved to the side); Node tests of the migration (9 cases). NOT CHECKED: the tablet pages (layout of the OBJECTS block and the PHONE CAM page with the preview was not drawn; sizes were taken from the existing rows), the phone link, the Quest. NOT BUILT (later): Option B, drawing props behind the DJ into the background pass so they show behind the cutout (needs GREEN SCREEN, one more small render). Also fixed in PROJECT.md: the Sound bullet had been cut at 'Opus stereo 16' and joined to the STREAM page bullet.
375. (16 Oct) HANDS ON THE SHOT + ROBO-BODY (owner: the robot or 3D hands must be able to overlay his own hands in the phone and headset shots; call the toggle ROBO-BODY). FOUND: both pcamPrep (PHONE CAM) and mrPrep (HEADSET CAM) already leave the drawn hands visible (since #288), following the single global controller look (xr.setCtlLook: 3D HANDS = skin hand, GLOVE = the robot DJ's glove, CONTROLLER); PHONE CAM also shows the forearm sleeves. There was no per-shot choice and no way to show the real hands bare. The ROBOT toggle only ever meant the full robot avatar, so greying it under HEADSET CAM / PHONE CAM (#373, #374) wrongly read as 'no robot hands'. BUILT (main.js, stream.js): (1) ROBOT renamed ROBO-BODY on the OBJECTS and STREAM pages and in the notes: the full robot avatar, drawn only in the DJ-camera shots (FRONT / FADERS / GOPRO); greyed under HEADSET CAM and PHONE CAM. (2) HANDS ON THE SHOT row on OBJECTS, shown with HEADSET CAM and with PHONE CAM (under the PHONE CAM block): REAL HANDS / 3D HANDS / ROBOT GLOVE. (3) REAL HANDS (showOpt.handsReal, default off, saved with the other SHOWTIME! settings): handsHides() leaves st.hand, st.grip, st.ctlHand.root and xr.glove.root out of the HEADSET CAM and PHONE CAM renders only (the same objects the DJ camera hides), and the PHONE CAM sleeves are not shown; the headset view keeps its look. FIRST PERSON and the other presets do not change it. (4) 3D HANDS and ROBOT GLOVE clear handsReal and set the controller look (xr.setCtlLook, also updating the start-page selector), so the headset shows the same hands as the shot; both are greyed with no headset. The bare CONTROLLER look has no button here (use CONTROLLERS on the start page / DJ TOOLS); with it nothing in the row is lit. NOT BUILT: an independent look per shot (e.g. viewers see the robot glove while the headset shows 3D hands): the glove is only posed while it is the active look, so both would have to be posed every frame; cost not measured. CHECKED: node --check; eslint no-undef (no undefined names in either file); Node tests of handsHides with mock hands (7 cases: all seven objects hidden, tip dots untouched, restore, off, already-hidden objects, no headset, missing glove / 3D hand) and of the wiring (pcamPrep, sleeves, mrPrep call it; the DJ camera path does not). NOT CHECKED: how the row looks on the tablet, and anything on the Quest or the phone.
376. (16 Oct) CLY3DTV, THE VENUE SCREEN (owner: the TV shows the code and the tablet types it; one TV; a tablet toggle for sending the sound; the TV page shows a small sound button only while sound is being delivered; page is venue.html, titled Cly3DTV). DESIGN: the TV page is the caller, the Quest the answerer, over the same ntfy.sh relay + WebRTC as the phone link. PAIRING: venue.html shows a 6-digit code (crypto random, renewed every 10 min) and listens on topic cly3dj-v1-tvpair-<code>; the tablet types the code (keypad on the CLY3DTV page); the Quest makes a 24-char random key, starts listening on cly3dj-v1-tvhost-<key>, posts {PAIR, key} to the code's topic; the TV keeps the key (localStorage cly3dtv.key) and calls the Quest; the pairing counts when that offer arrives (45 s limit; on failure the old pairing, if any, is restored and a message says no TV answered). Only a well-formed key from a PAIR message is accepted. A paired TV calls again by itself every time (retry every 3 s, 12 s per attempt); FORGET TV on the tablet sends {forget} and clears the key, the TV then shows a new code; NEW CODE on the TV does the same from its side. PICTURE: the Quest's answer sends the SAME video track Twitch gets (this.vTrack when live, else _videoTrack(source): the stream canvas, composed once, so no extra render, compose or read-back; only a second H.264 encode, capped 6 Mbit/s 30 fps). It works off air: when the TV connects and nothing is running the shot starts (setRehearse) and stops again when the TV leaves, unless PREVIEW (env.monitorOn) or going live wants it; PREVIEW off while the TV is up does not stop the shot. The canvas track is fed (requestFrame) whenever `sending` (live or TV up); Twitch teardown no longer stops the canvas track or the sound tap while the TV is up; switching source re-sends the picture to the TV (_tvFollow). SOUND: the master track Twitch gets (engine.streamTrack, after the limiter, no CUE, with the PHONE CAM latency hold-back), Opus about 160 kbit/s, one extra audio encode, no extra mixing. SOUND TO TV (tablet) uses replaceTrack and tells the TV over the ctl channel ({t:'audio', on}); venue.js shows the small SOUND button only while audio is on; the video starts muted (browsers need a key press), the first OK / Enter unmutes and stays unmuted for the visit, so a later sound-on is heard at once. TABLET: STREAM page third button CLY3DTV (lit while the TV is up) opens TOOLS > STREAM > CLY3DTV: unpaired view = code boxes + keypad + PAIR; paired view = TV ON/OFF, SOUND TO TV, FORGET TV (two taps), status line (TV connected, Mbit/s, fps, sound, 'shot runs off air'). FILES (new): venue.html, js/venue.js; changed: js/net-link.js (tvPairSend, tvPairListen, tvConnect, tvHost; a closed control channel counts as 'the other side is gone' on both ends so a closed page or a switched-off Quest is noticed at once), js/stream.js, js/main.js, sw.js (venue.html and venue added to the saved files). CHECKED (Node, real WebRTC via @roamhq/wrtc, in-process stand-in for ntfy.sh): link and pairing 15 checks (right / wrong code, malformed key, picture and sound tracks arrive, messages both ways, sound off / on, source swap, wrong key never reaches the Quest, a second call replaces the first); Streamer 23 checks (state machine, off-air shot start / stop, PREVIEW interplay, Twitch teardown, FORGET, restart, failed pairing restores the old key); venue.js 16 checks (code shown, pairing, live, sound button appears only with sound, first OK unmutes, off / on, Quest gone / back, forget, restart with a saved or damaged key); node --check and eslint no-undef on main.js and stream.js. A bug found by the tests and fixed: the TV only noticed a closed Quest after the slow connection timeout. NOT CHECKED: the real ntfy.sh relay, a Fire Stick / Silk browser (H.264 decode, autoplay, wake lock, the remote's OK key), an Android phone with HDMI, the Quest (encode cost of the second stream is unmeasured; the page shows Mbit/s and fps only), how the tablet page looks (keypad and rows were not drawn), host candidates over mDNS between the Quest and the TV browser, and venue Wi-Fi with client isolation (no relay fallback, so it will not connect there). One TV only: a second would be a second encode. NOT BUILT: a separate source for the TV (it shows what Twitch gets), a short address for venue.html, several TVs.
377. (16 Oct) OWNER FIXES: CHALKBOARD / CHAT PANEL SPLIT, DOME OBJECT, VISUALS ON VJ TOOLS, NATURAL HANDS ON PHONE CAM. (1) Chalkboard and chat panel overlapped and spawned at the same place: they were ONE ChatPanel object that flipped between chat and slate (setBoard), so they shared a position and could not be out together. Now two objects from one class (chat.js: ChatPanel(rig, home, {key, boardOnly, name})): the CHAT PANEL (chat only, never builds the slate canvases) and the CHALKBOARD (always the slate), each with its own saved place (vire.chatPanel, vire.chalkboard) and own start spot: panel left of the mixer (-0.75 m, yaw 0.94 as before), chalkboard right of it (+0.75 m, yaw -0.94), so both can be out at once. Migration: a chalkboard saved by the old single panel (board true) moves to vire.chalkboard (same place, size, on) and the panel key is cleared. Every chalkboard use in main.js now points at the new `chalkboard` object (tablet buttons CHALKBOARD / WIPE, double-tap = back to its spot, the XR writing loop, grabbable props, the test hook); the SHOWTIME! toggles CHAT PANEL and CHALKBOARD now each hide only their own object (propHides), and the phone shot's 'behind the DJ' rule covers the chalkboard too. A chat-only panel's touch() is now safe (it had no stroke map). (2) DOME is an object on the SHOWTIME! OBJECTS page (greyed under HEADSET CAM): it is the backdrop's 'dome' choice, so on = DOME and off = the VISUALS picture behind (they are one at a time; the dome would hide it); turning it on with no sky picked still switches and says to pick one (VISUALS > PANO > SKY). NONE stays on the STREAM page's BACK button. (3) VJ TOOLS has a VISUALS button (both layouts, next to SCROLLER) that opens the VISUALS pages, the same as VJ SETUP on the STREAM page. Leaving the VISUALS pages returns to the mixer page, not back to VJ TOOLS. (4) PHONE CAM shows your natural hands only (owner: 3D / robot hands were too hard to line up): pcamPrep forces handsHides (hands, grips, 3D-hand roots, glove) and never shows the sleeves; the HANDS row on the PHONE CAM block is removed (it stays for HEADSET CAM, where REAL HANDS / 3D HANDS / ROBOT GLOVE still apply). Supersedes the PHONE CAM part of #375. CHECKED (Node): t_split 31 checks with real three.js and real canvases (migration cases, the two objects, no overlap with a 1.25 m gap, saves and restarts, each toggle hides only its object, DOME tap logic, the VISUALS buttons, natural hands wiring); hands 11, behind-the-DJ 11, migration 9, TV link 15, streamer 23, venue page 16; node --check and eslint no-undef clean on main.js, stream.js and chat.js. A hazard found by the tests and fixed (touch() on the chat-only panel). NOT CHECKED: the tablet pages (layout of the new buttons and the ninth OBJECTS item was not drawn), the headset (where the chalkboard now spawns relative to the crate / decks on the right, which I could not see; grab it and the place is saved), the phone shot.
378. (16 Oct) CLY3DTV CONNECT BUTTON IN LANDSCAPE, GEAR LOCKDOWN, 45 DEGREE DECK TURNING, LANDSCAPE FIT OF THE NEW PAGES. (1) The CLY3DTV PAIR (Connect) button did not show in landscape: the tablet canvas is 768 x 304 there and the page stacked title, code boxes, keypad and PAIR down the screen, so PAIR started at y 314. Landscape now has two columns (instruction, code boxes and PAIR on the left, the keypad on the right: PAIR at y 216 to 260). Portrait unchanged. (2) GEAR LOCKDOWN (DJ TOOLS, both layouts): ON at every start, not saved. While on, deckA, deckB and the mixer can't be picked up or turned by the pointer / mouse path (main.js, before stage.beginMove) or by the headset hand-grab path (xr.js, before beginMove; a buzz and a message 'GEAR LOCKDOWN is on' once every 2.5 s). Flight cases, the crate and the lid handle, milk crates, neon and the LED wall are not locked; RESET LAYOUT still works. (3) The turntables turn in 45 degree steps (decks only, keys deckA / deckB): xr.js snapStep, applied to the hand-twist yaw while a deck is held (it lines up to the nearest step at the first frame; a twist of more than 30 degrees = 22.5 plus a 7.5 degree detent moves to the next step with a buzz, so no wobble at the halfway point); the thumbstick turn feeds the same ratchet; desktop mouse wheel = one 45 degree step, Shift-drag snaps. Battle mode (both decks turned 90) is two clicks each. It needs GEAR LOCKDOWN off. (4) A test harness that runs the real drawToolsPage code on a mock canvas of the real sizes (t_layout: with() over a Proxy scope, buttons recorded through VP_HIT, texts through wrapText) found more landscape problems of mine: on SHOWTIME! OBJECTS the PHONE CAM block (GREEN SCREEN / LATENCY / LIGHT / SETUP, at y 342) and the HEADSET CAM HANDS row (y 332) were below the screen. OBJECTS in landscape is now compact (30 px rows, no captions, presets and the render-time readout on one row, short notes); the PHONE CAM page's help text is capped to the screen; and FORGET TV no longer reads 'TAP AGAIN' for the first 3 s after the page loads (tvForgetArm guard). Known, older than this work: the long help text under the STREAM page's buttons starts below the 304 px screen in landscape (all its buttons are visible). CHECKED (Node): t_378 24 checks (ratchet incl. wrap-round, desktop steps, lock keys, both grab paths wired, only decks snap); t_layout 88 checks over CLY3DTV (unpaired, pairing, paired x landscape / portrait), DJ TOOLS, OBJECTS for each camera, PHONE CAM page, STREAM page; earlier suites unchanged and passing (split 31, hands 11, behind-the-DJ 11, migration 9, TV link 15, streamer 23, venue page 16); node --check and eslint no-undef clean on main.js, xr.js, stream.js, chat.js. The lint caught lockBtn being declared in the portrait branch only (landscape would have crashed); fixed. NOT CHECKED: any of it on the headset or the tablet itself (the harness uses estimated text widths, 7.2 px per character). NOT BUILT YET, needs the owner's decision (owner asked 16 Oct): (a) a very thin kinematic box collider on the loose record sleeve (it clips decks and mixer) that still lets records enter it; (b) a snug kinematic collider for the tablet remote (it clips the decks). The app has no physics (#207 removed it on purpose: bounce and fall went through real furniture); sleeves and the tablet follow the hand with no collision, and gear surfaces are boxes found with footprints / settleStack. Proposed: no physics engine, a push-out of the held item's oriented box against the gear boxes (decks, mixer, cases, crates), sleeve box 0.315 x 0.315 x 0.003 m with its slot edge left open for records; the hand keeps going, the item stays outside. Questions: what should happen when the hand goes through gear (item stops at the surface, or slides along it); do records pass through the sleeve box only at its opening edge; and which gear is solid for the tablet.
379. (17 Oct) VISUALS > FX: FLOAT, WATER, PANO SPIN (owner: some video panos stand in shallow water; a float object to drag onto gear, a water-ripple refraction plane, and an auto-rotate pano slider 0 to 5; asked first whether the shaders hurt the Quest since they go out to CLY3DTV and Twitch). ANSWER GIVEN: FLOAT has no GPU cost (CPU transforms only); true refraction (a copy of the scene behind the water, like three's transmission) would be a whole extra scene render per eye plus the stream camera, so it was NOT built that way; the water is the cheap game version (one transparent plane + the gear's own shaders), estimated 0.1 to 0.4 ms a frame depending on screen coverage; PANO SPIN is free. BUILT: new js/fx.js (in sw.js APP). (1) FLOAT: FX tab FLOAT + puts a see-through blue ball 42 cm in front of the DJ. It is on USER_LAYER 6, which only the desktop camera and the headset's eye cameras draw (enabled on renderer.xr.getCamera() and its cameras every frame; three r169 does not copy the main camera's mask to them), so AVACAM, GoPro, SHOWTIME!, HEADSET CAM, CLY3DTV and Twitch never see the ball or its slider. Grip / pinch carries it (generic ctx.props path); let go over gear and it sticks to the smallest piece whose box holds it (decks, mixer, record case, milk crates, neon, LED wall, flight case, a record on a deck, a loose record, a loose sleeve; one float per piece, a new ball takes over). A slider (12 cm, trigger / pinch, new xr.js propKnob hook: pr.knob / knobMove / knobEnd) floats 7.5 cm over the ball, turned to the DJ, with a label (e.g. FLIGHT CASE 50%). Grip the ball twice within 0.45 s = removed. Motion per piece: bob 2 cm + 0.7 cm, roll / pitch about 3 deg, slow yaw 1.7 deg and 1.2 cm drift, x the slider value; big pieces move slower and tilt less (k = sqrt(0.5 / size)). Riders: anything whose visible-mesh box bottom is within 4 cm of the piece's top and whose centre lies over it rides along, transitively (a floated case carries both decks and the mixer; a milk crate carries the record case); refreshed every 0.5 s; a rider that is being carried is skipped. The float is put on in fx.preRender (frame(), just before stepDjCam / streamer / render, so every camera sees it) and taken off in fx.postRender right after renderer.render, so hands, grabs, settling, colliders and the saved layout only ever see the real pose; a piece that moves eases its float to 0 and back 0.5 s after it is still. deckInst.update() runs again after the float so the instanced deck parts follow. Boxes use VISIBLE meshes only (Box3.setFromObject counted a hidden glow sprite: decks measured 0.48 to 1.48 m tall and nothing registered as riding on the case). Saved: vire.fx.floats (stage / case pieces only: key, value, offset); records and sleeves are not saved. Cost measured on the PC: 0.14 ms a frame CPU with two floats (one carrying three riders). (2) WATER: FX tab WATER (tap on / off, double-tap = back round the flight case), WATER HERE, sliders WATER LEVEL (-30 to 120 cm), SIZE (keeps the width / depth ratio), RIPPLE, MURK, REFRACT, CAUSTICS, CLEAR. The plane (layer 0, so streams and TV get it): five travelling waves + two drifting ring ripples as analytic slopes, fresnel (0.1 floor) reflection of the current panorama (same direction-to-uv as skybox.js, left-eye half, skybox group rotation inverted, so it follows TURN and PANO SPIN), deep teal body vec3(0.03, 0.16, 0.19), sun glints, a sheen on the ripples, 18 cm soft edges; in passthrough with no pano it reflects a grey-blue gradient. Gear under the surface (inside the plane's rectangle): onBeforeCompile injection into built-in gear materials only (MeshStandard / Physical / Lambert / Phong / Basic with no onBeforeCompile of their own; records, the crate disc and sleeve discs are skipped, so the groove shader is untouched): vertices lifted by 1/4 of their depth (apparent depth through water) and a small wobble; fragments tinted, murk fog by depth, moving caustic lines (own pattern), a faint waterline. Uniforms shared by every injected material; off = uFxWater.x 0, a uniform branch only. Materials are injected the first time WATER goes on in a session (one recompile, a short hitch: the toast says so) and new gear is picked up by a scan every 3 s while it is on. Handles (user layer only): a post with a blue ring at the near edge (grip carries the water, it stays level, only its yaw follows the hand) and a post with a silver cube on the corner (trigger / pinch: width and depth from the hand). Saved: vire.fx.water. Tuning on the PC: the first look was almost invisible (body uTint * 0.22, F floor 0.02, CLEAR 0.7); now body deep teal, F floor 0.1, CLEAR 0.45 default. (3) PANO SPIN: VISUALS > PANO, slider 0 to 5 degrees a second in 0.25 steps (landscape: between the SKY buttons and the LED button; portrait: its own row, the PANO page shows 8 thumbnails instead of 10). applySky's turn and key-light lines moved into applySkyTurn(), called every frame while the spin runs; the key light, scene.environmentRotation / backgroundRotation, the DOME and the PHONE CAM / LIGHT MATCH key direction all add skySpin.ang. Stopping leaves the pano where it got to; the saved per-picture TURN is not changed. Saved: vire.skySpin. VISUALS tabs: FX added (landscape tabs narrower, SCROLLER 80 px; portrait SCROLLER moved to the third column). CHECKED on the owner's PC (browser pane, RTX 4050, localhost:8000): node --check and eslint no-undef clean (main.js, xr.js, fx.js); floats attach, restore after reload, ride-along (case -> deck A, deck B, mixer); water plane, reflection and underwater gear seen in a render with skytest_mono.jpg (copied to web/_test/ for the test, not shipped); FX page and PANO SPIN fit landscape and portrait; spin measured ~2 deg/s at 2.5 (timer jitter). NOT CHECKED: anything in the headset (grabbing the ball, the slider, the water handles, eye-camera layer, Quest frame time with WATER on). Known limits: hands and grabs use the unfloated pose, so a floated deck's platter / faders are up to ~2.5 cm off where they are drawn (fine for props, odd for scratching a floating deck); the contact shadows under gear don't bob; FLOAT has no desktop mouse drag (test hooks: vire.fx.spawnBall / attach / tryAttach).
380. (18 Oct) S8 STUDIO (owner's core redesign: 3DTV and Twitch run from the spectator phone, a Samsung S8 with Chrome 101, later an S22 / S24). WHY: the Quest rendered the shot, read it back, encoded it twice (Twitch + Fire Stick TV) and uploaded to Twitch. Now it draws only the scene and sends one packed stream to the S8, which adds the backdrop and its own camera, shows the result full screen over HDMI and goes live on Twitch. OWNER DECISIONS: build for the S8 (Chrome 101.0.4951.61); fixed (tripod) camera mode for the live modes; the S8 does NONE / DOME / the LED clip from its own copy, anything else the Quest bakes into its frame; the Quest sends the Twitch key to the S8 over the link; keep both old paths (Quest-direct Twitch and the Fire Stick venue.html); 4.5 Mbit/s Quest to S8 (club Wi-Fi), 720p to Twitch and TV. BUILT: (1) s8test.html: one-page S8 capability test (stand-in packed scene H.264 4.5 Mbit/s through an in-page loopback, rear camera 1280 x 720, WebGL2 key + composite full screen, 720p 3.5 Mbit/s re-encode; fps, encoder / decoder names, quality limit reason, 10-minute graph and verdict); heavier than the real studio by one encode + decode. (2) net-link.js: the phone's offer gets two receive slots (video, H.264 preferred; audio), Opus stereo 160 kbit/s asked for in the phone's offer (opusStereo); the Quest's answer turns those m-lines (recvMids) into send slots in one stream (lip sync); Pipe.setStudio(v, a, kbps) / hostLink.studioOk, setStudio. Old phone pages simply have no slots (the tablet says reload). (3) stream.js: STUDIO state 'vire.studio' {on, mode show | dj | key, snd, trim}; studio ON closes the Fire Stick link, turns PHONE CAM / PHONE sources into SHOWTIME!, and GO LIVE sends {k:'stuLive', key} to the S8 (state follows the S8's 'sst' reports; a 20 s no-answer timeout); OFF = everything as before. Packed frame 1920 x H (_stuPack): colour 1280 wide premultiplied over black (2D canvas, see-through scene drawn on black), a timestamp strip x 1284-1292 (28 blocks of (H-24)/28 px: 1, 0, 24 bits of the Quest's ms clock, 2 bits of the bit count; stampBits / readStamp), matte 624 wide at x 1296 (the layer's alpha as grey via copy / source-in white / destination-over black). Overlays (scroller, now playing, logo, chat) go in the pack with their alpha. SHOW: the existing SHOWTIME! layer (_layerFrame, 960 x 540) packed in _compose; its camera pose goes to the S8 each frame ('state' {k:'scp', t, p, q, fov}) for the dome; VISUALS are baked (opaque frame) unless the S8 reports the same LED clip playing (env.ledClip vs sst.clip); HEADSET CAM, CARD and AVACAM go opaque as they are. LIVE DJ! / LIVE KEY! (_stuPhoneTick): the scene from the S8's calibrated pose (phoneCam: p, q, ff, vw, vh) on a see-through background with the PHONE CAM rules (pcamPrep: no room / sky / wall, Option A props behind the DJ, natural hands), 1280 x H, read back async, packed. No latency hold-back on the Quest (the S8 delays its camera instead). active / sending include the studio; wantsCam is off in the live modes. (4) studio.js (new, the S8 side, inside the camera page): replaces the mirror render with one composite drawn to the screen at 1280 x 720 (renderer pixel ratio 1, CSS contain, page UI hidden): backdrop NONE (gradient) / DOME (this phone's skybox rendered from the shot's camera into a half-float target: the S8's pose in LIVE KEY!, the Quest's 'scp' pose in SHOW) / VISUALS (this phone's LED clip, cover crop); camera plain (LIVE DJ!) or keyed (LIVE KEY!: vs.K colour, strength, softness, spill, crop, colour match on DOME); the Quest's scene over it (c = s + c (1 - a), matte cleaned 3 %); an opaque Quest frame is shown as it is (fit). SYNC: each Quest frame's strip is read back (32 x 1, async) and compared with the Quest clock (link ping / pong) = how late it is (D, smoothed); the camera keeps a ring of 12 frames (1280 x 720 copies, ~400 ms) tagged with capture time (rvfc captureTime, else presentationTime - 15) in the Quest's clock, and the composite uses the one filmed at now - D - TRIM. Sound: the Quest's master in the same WebRTC stream as its picture (in step); played by an <audio> element (HDMI) after START STUDIO (full screen, landscape lock, wake lock, sound need a tap); forwarded to Twitch. Twitch: WHIP from the S8 (same endpoint and stereo munging as the Quest), canvas captureStream 3.5 Mbit/s 30 fps + the Quest's audio track; 3 reconnects; keeps going for 60 s if the Quest link drops (shows the last frame). Status to the Quest once a second ({k:'sst', run, fps, qfps, hold, d, tw, twMsg, kbps, note, clip}); status box on the S8 (tap to show), 'Camera view (pause studio here)' and '▶ BACK TO STUDIO'. Composite capped at ~30 fps. The tablet's PREVIEW monitor shows the S8's own output (its preview JPEGs, sent while PREVIEW is on). (5) Tablet: TOOLS > STREAM > the screen button reads CLY3DTV or S8 STUDIO and opens that page; new page S8 STUDIO (both layouts): STUDIO ON / OFF, SOUND, BACK (the shared NONE / VISUALS / DOME), SHOW / LIVE DJ! / LIVE KEY!, DELAY -10 / TRIM (tap = 0) / DELAY +10, SHOW CAMERA ▸ (OBJECTS page), status (S8 fps, Quest frames fps, camera hold, Twitch); CLY3DTV page has S8 STUDIO ▸, the studio page FIRE STICK ▸; STREAM status line adds 'VIA S8 <mode>'; picking PHONE CAM while the studio is on says to use LIVE DJ! / LIVE KEY!. (6) vset.js: ensure and playPano exported (the S8's DOME without the AR virtual set). sw.js += studio. CHECKED: node --check on all files; stamp round trip; cloud headless Chromium (SwiftShader, VP8) real WebRTC between a Quest Streamer and the S8 studio through an in-page ntfy stand-in: slots negotiate, picture and sound arrive, SHOW / LIVE DJ! / LIVE KEY! composite correctly (screenshots); owner's PC (browser pane, RTX 4050) same harness (web/_test/studio-test.html): Quest packs at 27 fps, Quest frames 65-100 ms late, camera held the same; a moving bar filmed by the stand-in camera and the same bar drawn by the Quest line up within ~40-55 ms (the stand-in camera has no captureTime; TRIM covers the rest); the real camera page (index.html?role=camera) enters the studio screen with no errors. NOT CHECKED: the S8 itself (Chrome 101, H.264 hardware encode / decode, heat, HDMI mirroring: Android 9 mirrors the 18.5:9 screen, so the TV picture has bars until a DeX phone), the Quest's frame rate with the packing on, real ntfy pairing of the new offer size, Twitch from the S8, the DOME's match to the Quest's (PANO SPIN is not sent to the phone), light wrap (not in the S8 key). NOT BUILT: several screens, the DOME's PANO SPIN, light wrap / glow on the S8 key.
381. (18 Oct) S8 TEST RESULTS + FIX (owner ran s8test.html on the S8, the M33 and the laptop). S8 (Chrome 101): hardware H.264 encode and decode (ExternalEncoder / MediaCodecVideoDecoder), composite 19.9 fps after 10 min (worst 8), camera 14-17 fps, 'Quest stream frames 0 fps'. M33 (Chrome 155): c2.exynos.h264 encoder both ways, MediaCodec decode, 15.4 fps after 10 min, camera 10-12 fps, Quest frames 8-10 fps. Laptop: 83 fps (AMD H.264), camera 15 fps. READING: both phones were held back by the page's main thread (the test draws the 1920 x 720 stand-in scene in 2D every frame, which the real S8 never does, plus one extra encode and decode); the camera rates are the cameras slowing down in a dim room (all three devices under 30, the laptop too). FOUND: Chrome 101 on the S8 never fires requestVideoFrameCallback for a WebRTC video (decoder busy, 0 callbacks), and three's VideoTexture waits for those callbacks, so the studio would have shown no Quest picture on the S8. FIXED studio.js: with no callback for 400 ms the studio polls the video's clock (currentTime moved = a new frame, texture flagged by hand, the timestamp strip read). s8test.html: same fallback for its frame count; new START (REAL LOAD): the stand-in drawn once into 16 pictures and only copied, composite capped at 30 fps like the studio, so the result is close to the real load (it still has the extra stand-in encode). Not yet re-run on the phones.
382. (18 Oct) LOW LIGHT for the S8 STUDIO camera (owner: hold 25 fps rather than 30, and raise the exposure; club light will be typical or darker). The s8test runs showed every camera slowing to 10-17 fps in a dim room (it lengthens each frame's exposure). BUILT: fixedcam.js asks for frameRate {ideal 25, min 24} when LOW LIGHT 25 is on (default on; falls back to ideal 25 if the camera has no 24+ range, checked in Chromium: the fake camera refuses min 24 and the fallback opens it), applies exposure compensation EV (default +1, clamped to the camera's range) after every open; the phone's own Exp -/+ buttons now keep their value; both kept as 'vire.fixcam.low'; setLowLight({fps25, ev}) reopens the camera only when the fps choice changes; camInfo() = the camera's real frame rate / EV / max. studio.js: GAIN in the composite on the S8 camera only (1 - (1 - c)^gain: lifts darks and mids, highlights stay; 1 = as filmed), applied before the key; the measured camera rate goes to the tablet in 'sst'.cam and on the S8's status box. stream.js STUDIO_DEF += fps25 true, ev 1 (-2 to +3, 0.5 steps), gain 1.2 (1 to 2.5, 0.1 steps), sent in the 'stu' message as cam. Tablet S8 STUDIO page: a LOW LIGHT row (25 FPS: ON / OFF, EV -, EV value (tap = 0), EV +, GAIN -, GAIN value (tap = 1), GAIN +) and the line 'S8 camera: N fps, EV x (max y)', amber with a hint when it runs under 22 fps; landscape rows 30 px high so it all fits (checked on the PC, both layouts). NOT CHECKED: whether Chrome 101 on the S8 really holds 25 fps (Android may still pick a 15-30 range; the tablet line shows the real rate), EV range on the S8, noise at high EV / GAIN.

## Findings
- (26 Sep, tooling) Committing files from the cloud workspace: re-committing from the same staged path can silently write the previous upload. Stage each round under a fresh folder name and check md5 on the device after every commit. PNGs arrive with an extra caBX (content credentials) chunk, so compare their decoded pixels, not the file md5.
- 26 Sep: APK root `lib/arm64-v8a` has libfmod.so, libfmodstudio.so and libresonanceaudio.so. VRL audio is FMOD + Resonance spatial audio.
- 26 Sep: Demo Tracks.m3u lists 11 tracks; only 4 Deacoy MP3s are in the folder. Local testing only, never shipped.

- 26 Sep: `ViRE_rekordbox.xml` (Rekordbox 7.2.16, via Rekopy): 720 tracks, 10 playlists (incl. "B Side Dubs"). 719 relative `Collection/<TrackID> - <title> - <artist>.mp3`, 1 absolute `file://` path (won't resolve). 718 MP3 + 2 WAV; 711 at 44.1k, 9 at 48k.
- 26 Sep: Every track has a TEMPO beatgrid (Inizio = beat time, Battito = beat in bar); multiple TEMPO rows are grid re-anchors, BPM never varies within a track. 296 tracks carry hot cues (POSITION_MARK Num>=0, with RGB). Tonality + Genre present for the crate screen.
- 26 Sep: Rekopy renames files to `<TrackID> - <title> - <artist>`, so an original filename suffix like `_b_s` is lost unless it's in the Rekordbox title.
- 27 Sep: Quest 3's 3.5 mm jack is headphone OUTPUT only (no TRRS mic input). External mics/line sources go in over USB-C (USB audio class device), with Settings > Advanced > External Microphone enabled and a restart (Antlion setup guide). A phone's headphone out is ~0.5-1 V vs mic-level ~10 mV: needs a ~30 dB attenuator (with DC block, the mic pin carries bias) into a USB-C TRRS dongle (mono), or a class-compliant USB line-in interface (stereo). Unverified on Quest Browser: whether enumerateDevices lists internal and USB inputs separately.
- 27 Sep (turntable physics research, owner's KAB 'Study of Cogging Torque in Technics SL-1200 Turntables' PDF + specs): MK2 motor = 3-phase brushless DC with iron stator, 16-pole ring magnet, 12 slots; torque set by a DC control voltage; bi-directional (can pull back = electronic brake, 45->33 change near instant). Speed control = quartz PLL on a frequency-generator coil round the magnet: 50.55 Hz at 33 1/3 (= 91 pulses/rev, one pulse = 3.96 deg = 19.8 ms of audio), speed+phase signals through an integrator and ~300 ms RC filter; response to a load change ~300 ms (1/6 rev) on MK2, ~1 s on the pre-1979 original. Spec: 0% speed change for load torque up to 0.8 lb-in (~0.09 N m), i.e. integral action, zero steady-state error below that. Starting torque 1.5 kg cm (0.147 N m), build-up 0.7 s, platter 2.0 kg with rubber mat (MK7: 0.18 N m, 0.7 s, 1.8 kg incl. slipmat, coreless = no cogging, 4 torque/brake levels). Measured with a gram gauge: bearing friction alone 1.5 g at the platter edge (6 in) ~0.0022 N m; bearing + cogging 3.5 g at the edge; cogging alone ~2 g at the edge (~0.003 N m peak), 48 cogging steps/rev (26.64 Hz at 33 1/3), rumble -56 dB = inaudible. Hand-turned slowly and released, an MK2 platter comes to a near stop and rocks back slightly (detent). Author's summary: low running torque motors, easily stopped by hand at normal speed. Implication: bearing friction alone would coast ~40+ s from 33 1/3, so the current 7 s power-off coast (#48) needs a real measurement before changing.
- 27 Sep (tooling): the cloud sandbox has 2 CPUs and software WebGL, so a scripted full-app browser test crawls (~1 fps, timers starved; #120 lost ~30 min to it). Test worklet/physics changes in Node by loading deck-worklet.js in a vm with stubbed sampleRate / registerProcessor / AudioWorkletProcessor (harness kept in the session scratchpad only); for a browser smoke test use a 320x200 viewport and call vire.renderer.setAnimationLoop(null) before measuring. Visual/feel checks belong on the owner's PC / Quest.
- 27 Sep (tooling): the device shell (device_bash) is a Linux VM on the owner's laptop with 2 CPUs and no GPU, and it kills background jobs when the call ends; run long CPU jobs (AO bake) in the cloud container. The owner's own GPU (RTX 4050, Windows 11, 16 GB) is reachable through the Claude desktop app's built-in browser pointed at his running server (http://localhost:8000/vire/index.html or the Pinggy URL): use that for visual checks instead of headless swiftshader renders.
- 27 Sep (tooling): Blender Lab MCP server (official, Blender Foundation, experimental) connected: add-on mcp-1.0.3 in Blender 5.1.2 on the owner's laptop + .mcpb extension in the Claude desktop app; its tools reach the cloud session as mcp__remote-devices__Blender__* (execute_blender_code, window/area screenshots, render_viewport_to_path, scene summaries, API docs search). Cycles compute set to OptiX on the RTX 4050 (CUDA and CPU unticked). The server runs LLM-written Python with no guard: work only in ViRe_Custom\blender\ and never open unrelated .blend files.

## Planned first build (v0)
- Single self-contained HTML file: Three.js + WebXR (immersive-ar passthrough, immersive-vr fallback, desktop preview).
- Audio: Web Audio API, one AudioWorklet per deck reading from a single playhead at variable rate (platter speed drives rate, so scratching works).
- Two decks: platter, strobe dots, start/stop, 33/45, pitch fader (±8%, green zero LED), tonearm.
- Mixer: trim, 3-band EQ, filter, cue, LED meters, channel faders, crossfader, SYNC button in the centre column, per-channel live BPM readout.
- Track loading via file picker; BPM from Rekordbox XML when available, else detected on load (steady-tempo assumption).
- Fake bloom with emissive materials + glow sprites (no post-processing in XR).
- Snap Specs notes: additive see-through display (dark = invisible), small FOV, test performance early.
- 26 Sep: web\rekordbox.xml = full Rekordbox 7.2.16 export: 734 tracks, 16 playlists, absolute file://localhost/ paths; 725 under D:/Music_Crates/, 9 under C:/Users/cly3d/. 720 overlap the Rekopy export. Using it directly needs a server mount for D:\Music_Crates plus a path-prefix map in the app (not done; owner asked for analysis only).

## Build plan (agreed order, 26 Sep)
(26 Sep revision: step 0 becomes local server + Pinggy check; steps 1-8 built desktop/mouse first; step 9 hand/controller input and XR sessions come after the desktop version works.)
0. Feasibility spike: tiny artifact that enters immersive-vr and immersive-ar on Quest Browser, opens a folder/file picker, reads a file, plays audio through an AudioWorklet. Go/no-go for hosting as a claude.ai artifact.
1. App shell + start screen: Full VR / Passthrough / Desktop preview buttons, gated by isSessionSupported. Scene, lighting, desktop orbit camera.
2. Library: open Rekopy export folder, parse XML (collection, playlists, TEMPO grid, POSITION_MARK cues, key, genre), resolve relative Collection/ paths, pair A/B by title suffix, flag _s, mark missing files. Folder handle / OPFS cache for reopening.
3. Audio engine: decode on demand, AudioWorklet deck player (variable-rate playhead, scratch, 33/45, +-16% pitch), pan/balance with _s mono fold, trim, 3-band EQ, filter, channel fader, crossfader, cue/headphone bus, meters.
4. SYNC: meet-in-the-middle using Rekordbox grid, fader glide, last-touched phase lead, one-beat nudge, refuse outside +-16%.
5. Deck models: procedural platter, plinth, strobe dots, start/stop, 33/45, pitch fader + zero LED, tonearm (behind swappable-model interface).
6. Record: groove shader driven by loudness envelope (lead-in, bands, run-out), needle radius follows playhead, labels from ID3 APIC art with optional title/BPM overlay, generated fallback label, hot-cue edge markers.
7. Mixer model: all knobs/faders/LEDs/SYNC/BPM readouts wired to step 3-4.
8. Crate: flight case with record sleeves and track screen (playlists, title, BPM, key, length, genre), browse and pull a record.
9. Interaction: hand tracking first (grab, flip A/B, drop on platter, spin/scratch, knobs, faders, buttons), controllers as bonus, desktop mouse.
10. Look pass: PBR materials, emissive + glow sprites, passthrough tuning, Snap Specs additive-display check.
11. Test and tune on Quest 3: perf, audio latency, memory with long tracks. Publish artifact, update this file.

## Open questions
- Is WebXR allowed inside a published claude.ai artifact, or do we host on GitHub Pages (HTTPS)?
- Sync when the average tempo falls outside ±8%: refuse, or switch to a wider range?
- Which deck leads phase alignment when meeting in the middle?
- (26 Sep) +-16: BPM or percent?
- (26 Sep) Instrumental/full positions: keep stereo width, or fold everything to mono?
- (26 Sep) Pseudo stem quality depends on dry centred vocals; decide how to handle tracks where it fails (quality meter? just accept bleed?).
- (26 Sep) How is a split (vocal L / instrumental R) file flagged: filename token, ID3 comment, or a toggle on the crate screen?
- (26 Sep) Crate persistence: re-pick files each session, or cache them in the artifact's IndexedDB so the crate survives restarts?
- (26 Sep) A/B pairing under Rekopy: put the `_a` / `_b` / `_b_s` suffix in the Rekordbox title (stripped for display), or pair consecutive tracks in a dedicated playlist like "B Side Dubs"?
- (26 Sep) Test early: can the artifact frame open a folder (showDirectoryPicker / webkitdirectory) on PC Chrome and Quest Browser? Fallback = multi-file pick, cached in OPFS/IndexedDB.
- (26 Sep) Which local port does the Pinggy tunnel forward, and where is the Rekopy Collection/ folder on the PC?

## Build log
- 26 Sep, desktop v0.1 in web\ (index.html + js\ + vendor\three r169, vendored locally; check.html = old server test page).
  - js/library.js: XML parse, A/B pairing by title suffix, _s flag, grid, hot cues, playlists (missing tracks sorted last). js/id3.js: APIC cover art.
  - js/deck-worklet.js: both decks in one AudioWorklet (Int16 PCM, cubic interpolation, motor accel/brake, +-16% pitch, 33/45, hand/scratch, needle gain, balance pan with mono fold for _s, phase nudge). js/audio.js: mixer graph (trim, 3-band EQ with kill, LP/HP filter knob, cue, faders, constant-power crossfader, master limiter, split cue L=master R=cue).
  - SYNC: meet-in-middle glide 0.4 s, then phase nudge led by last-touched pitch fader; max 25% rate bend, one beat when offset <= 0.25 beat. Headless test: both decks landed at 127.24 BPM, residual phase 0.0002 beat.
  - js/models.js + textures.js: procedural decks (strobe rows that stand still at 169/174/180/186-dot speeds), tonearm solved from groove radius, mixer, flight-case crate with screen and instanced sleeves. Record grooves = loudness-driven colour/roughness + anisotropy map (radial highlight). Labels from ID3 art, title/BPM overlay default on for sides without art, L toggles.
  - Desktop mouse only; XR buttons enter a static preview (no hand input yet).
- Next: owner test on PC, then Quest pass (hands/controllers, perf, texture sizes).

## #391 LIGHT PHONE for video panos (cly3dj-dev)
- VISUALS > PANO: a video pano now has LIGHT PHONE too. `lightFrame(name)` (main.js, above `lightPhone`) seeks the clip to frame 61 (2 s at 30 fps, past any fade-in; clamped for short clips), draws it to a 2048 px canvas and saves it with `media.importFile('Pano', ...)` as `<name> light frame.jpg`. `lightPhone` then sends that still to the phone as before (Fixed camera must be on). The frame is reused if it already exists.
- A video pano as the phone's background already worked: SKY on the Quest sends `m.media`, and vset.js plays the phone's own copy (copy files over with VISUALS > SYNC, tag VPANO).
- Backup: `backups/pre-391/main.js`. No server restart (static files).

## PARKED 18 Oct: signalling without ntfy.sh (revisit later)
- ntfy.sh only carries the handshake (net-link.js `RELAY`); after that the Quest and phone talk directly over the LAN. Google STUN is the only other internet piece and is not needed on one subnet.
- Backup idea (owner agreed in principle, not built): a "relay address" setting, ntfy.sh default, a free public MQTT broker over wss as the second path (needs a small MQTT client), or a Cloudflare Workers / Deno relay, or server.js behind Pinggy (PC must stay on).
- GitHub Pages cannot host a relay (static only). A true no-internet hotspot needs a local https relay (Termux on the M33 is possible); the open problem is https from the Quest to a LAN address. Test on the PC first.
- A phone hotspot with mobile data on works today with ntfy.sh (the handshake is a few KB).

## #393 FETCH ART in the Record Maker: Discogs + MusicBrainz (owner, 18 Oct) (cly3dj-dev)
- New `js/artfetch.js` (added to sw.js APP). RECORD MAKER > NAME row: FETCH ART… opens page `art`: SEARCH… (ARTIST - TITLE, prefilled from the picked song or the name), DISCOGS / MUSICBRAINZ toggles, FIND, results with thumbnails (vinyl pressings first, the two sources interleaved). Tap a release: page `artpick` with slots SLEEVE, LABEL A, BACK and the release's pictures; tap a slot then a picture (next slot is picked automatically; tapping the same picture again empties the slot); USE THIS ART fetches the full pictures.
- MusicBrainz: recording search (title + artist), its releases; pictures from the Cover Art Archive are typed (Front, Back, Medium = label) so all three slots fill themselves; release-group fallback. 1 request a second. No key.
- Discogs: database/search (track + artist, then q), releases/{id}; token as a query parameter (no CORS pre-flight). Pictures are untyped: primary = sleeve, next = back guess, label picked by hand. i.discogs.com sends no CORS header (fetch fails), so pictures go through https://wsrv.nl/?url=… (checked: 200, image/jpeg).
- Token: never in the code or repo. localStorage `vire.discogsToken`, set by opening the app once with `?discogs=TOKEN` (stripped from the address at once, history.replaceState), or TOKEN… on the FETCH ART page (paste with MIC / KEYS), or on the PC from `web/discogs.local.json` {"token": "..."} if the owner makes one (never copy that file to cly3dj-dev / cly3dj).
- Stamping: label = fetched LABEL A (wins over PICTURE…), else the picture; sleeve front and back saved as `<id>_cover.jpg` / `<id>_back.jpg` (1024 px square) in vire-press (tools.js savePressing extra, readPic, deletePressing removes them, labelFrom(file, S)). pressArt: cover = the crate sleeve / phone cover, label stays the disc label (labelArt), back = backBlobs (sleeve back).
- Maker preview disc shows the fetched label; LABEL sub-line reads "Art: sleeve ✓ label ✓ back ✓ · release". NONE also drops the fetched label.
- Test hooks: `vire._art` (art, maker, openArtPage, runArtSearch, openArtRelease, useArt, pressRecord, plNow).
- Tested in the PC pane: Madonna - Ray of Light: 48 results; MB release 35e414a2 filled all three slots; Discogs 121732 (13 pictures via wsrv); stamped a blank with all three, the crate sleeve showed the fetched cover; test pressing removed afterwards. Untested in the headset.
- Backup: backups/pre-392/ (main.js, tools.js, sw.js, index.html). No server restart (static files).

Tap: `spawnMilk(true)` pops a new milk crate up beside the record crate (`placeMilk(m, side)` tries left / right first, then the old ring), the playlist's name is scrawled on its label (empty name = PLAYLIST), then every record of the playlist jumps out of the record crate one by one (0.3 s apart): Record3D standing in the crate, ballistic start speed solved for the loose-record air drag (v *= 1 - 0.5 dt) to land 10 cm over the milk crate after 0.55 to 0.8 s, random flip spin; `stepLoose` / `enterMilk` / the milk crate physics settle them. Records with all 3 copies out or missing files stay; at most 40. `stepPlJump(dt)` in the frame loop.

- Every milk crate gets a label on both long sides (0.21 x 0.08 m, y = MILK.H - 0.058, 4 mm off the wall): `attachMilkLabel(m, key)` for the first crate and in `newMilk`; a freshly spawned crate's label is cleared; `removeMilk` forgets and disposes it. Ink kept per crate as a PNG in localStorage `vire.milkLabel.<key>` (saved 1.5 s after the last stroke). `milkLabels()`.

## #395 WHITEBOARD with magnetic markers and duster (owner, 18 Oct) (cly3dj-dev)
- New `js/whiteboard.js`: `WhiteBoard extends ChatPanel` replaces the chalkboard's look (same object, key `vire.chalkboard`, same tablet buttons, renamed WHITEBOARD): white enamel with faint ghosts of old marker and duster smears, a brushed aluminium frame (22 mm face, 16 mm deep) with black corner caps, kept real size when the board is stretched (`_fitFrame`). No chalk ledge any more; fingertips no longer write.
- `makeBoardTools(board, rig)`: three magnetic marker pens (black, red, blue: white barrel, colour band, posted magnetic cap with clip, nib) stuck on the board's lower right, and a magnetic duster (felt to the board) on the lower left. Each is a prop for xr.js (grip / hands pinch). Markers have `penOff`: held like a pen, tip forward and 30 deg down from the grip, 3 cm out (xr.js now uses `pr.penOff` when present and calls `pr.grab(st)` so the prop knows the hand for the buzz). The duster keeps the pose it was grabbed in (felt still toward the board).
- Held marker tip within 4 mm of the board or a milk crate label writes (2 to 3 mm line, a little wider pressed in); the duster's felt within 12 mm rubs out (45 mm radius); a light buzz while writing / rubbing. Let go within 7 cm of the enamel: it sticks there (lies flat, turned to the board's plane); let go anywhere else: it flies back to its spot (0.45 s arc). Tools stay real size on a stretched board.
- Frame loop: `boardTools.tick(dt, [chalkboard, ...milkLabels()], xr)`, then `chalkboard.tickBoard()`, then each label's `tick()`. Props list: the tools come before the board, so a grip on a marker takes the marker.
- Tested in the PC pane: wrote a wave (black), a circle (red), a line (blue), the duster rubbed half the wave out, markers stuck where let go on the board, one let go 60 cm away flew home. Untested with real controllers / hands (the pen angle for bare hands follows the wrist and may need a tweak).

## #398 Playlist records jump into the milk crate IN THEIR SLEEVES, stacked like the record crate (owner, 18 Oct) (cly3dj-dev)
- No more bare discs from the PLAYLIST jump. Each milk crate holds a row of sleeved records (`mc.userData.sleeved`, up to 40): one InstancedMesh of plain card jackets per crate (shared `sleeveCard` material, one draw call, frustumCulled off) leaning back against the far wall (tilt -0.10 rad, 6.2 mm pitch, 7" jackets scaled like the record crate), and album covers on the 10 front-most only (`COVER_N` planes per crate, 256 px canvases via `drawJacket`, made only once a crate has sleeves). Owner's reason: GPU cost, same rule as the record crate.
- The jump (`launchSleeveToMilk`, `stepMilkFlyers`, called from `stepPlJump`): a sleeved record (one box, its cover on a 256 px canvas; pool of up to 6 reused flyers) rises out of the record crate, arcs over with one full flip, and drops into its reserved slot from 14 cm above; the slot stays hidden (`mc.userData.landing`) until it lands. 0.3 s apart; if no flyer is free it waits a frame.
- Take one out: grip (hands: pinch) on a sleeve's top edge in a milk crate (`milkSleeveHit`, top 10 cm of the crate to 6 cm above it, nearest slot along the lean): it becomes an ordinary lying sleeve in the hand (`sleeveGrab` milk branch; xr.js now accepts `h.milk`). Let a sleeve that still holds its record go over any milk crate: it stands in at the front of that row (`sleeveRelease`).
- A record standing in a milk crate counts as a copy out (`copiesOut`).
- Rows are kept per crate: localStorage `vire.milkSleeves.<key>` (file + title|artist items, like the playlists), restored after every library load (`restoreMilkSleeves`); cleared for a freshly spawned crate and forgotten when a crate is tossed away (its cover textures disposed).
- Fix during the test: covers sat inside the 3 mm jacket (offset 0.9 mm, copied from the record crate's thinner instances); now 2.1 mm in front.
- Tested in the PC pane (loop stepped by hand): 6 and then 20 records jumped into two new crates, 10 covers on the 20-crate, rows came back after a reload, a sleeve taken from one crate and let go over the other joined that row. Test crates and playlist removed afterwards. Untested in the headset.
- Backup: backups/pre-398/ (main.js, xr.js). No server restart (static files).

## #400 Whiteboard realism and dirrovinyl markers (owner, 18 Oct) (cly3dj-dev)
- whiteboard.js only. The board was an unlit screen (MeshBasicMaterial, toneMapped off); in board mode it is now `bMat`, a MeshPhysicalMaterial: map = the composed board, clear coat 0.45, roughness and clear-coat roughness from one gloss map (`rCanvas`, 512 x 640, roughness in green: enamel 26/255, wipe smears and cleaning swirls 50 to 110, dry ink 150, rubbed patches pulled toward 70), and an orange-peel bump (soft noise, tiled 5 x 6, bumpScale 0.35). The room and the neon now reflect in it and move as you walk. The chat panel keeps its unlit screen (`_scrMat0`).
- Ghosts: `bGhost` layer (composed between enamel and ink): starts with a few faint old marks; every duster pass copies 5 % of what it rubs into it, so ghosts build up. The tablet WIPE is a "spray clean": ink gone, ghosts 70 % gone, gloss map reset.
- Felt strokes (`feltSeg`): solid line, half width 1.9 mm + up to 1.1 mm pressed in; chisel width by direction (the nib's wide edge comes in as `extra.chisel`, world vector, from the held marker: 42 % along the edge, full across it); a darker pool where the pen lands or rests; fast strokes (> 1.1 px/ms) run dry in three streak lines that keep their place for the whole stroke (cut with butt ends back across the previous segment, so no beads or chain pattern at the joints, which the first two tries had).
- Markers: open dry-erase pens, no caps (owner). 13 cm: colour end plug with the owner's Gemini crest (models/avatar/gemini_emboss.png) as a bump map on its flat end, a printed white barrel (canvas label: "dirrovinyl" wordmark with a little record in a colour band, facing out of the board at u = 0 and on the far side, small print DRY ERASE · CHISEL TIP · colour, LOW ODOUR · NON-TOXIC · WHITEBOARDS & VINYL CRATES on the sides, a colour band by the plug, scuffs), tapered grip with four rings, colour collar, chisel felt nib. Satin plastic with a light clear coat.
- Tested in the PC pane: lit board with the neon's reflection and a highlight; slow / fast / chisel strokes; duster ghost; marker close-ups (label and crest). Untested in the headset. Cost: one extra 512 x 640 texture, uploaded with the board (up to ~15 a second while writing).
- Backup: backups/pre-400/whiteboard.js. No server restart (static files).

## #401 One switch per object across pages (owner, 18 Oct) (cly3dj-dev)
- SHOWTIME! OBJECTS no longer keeps stream-only copies of DOME, WHITEBOARD, CHAT PANEL and NEON. Those buttons now switch the object itself (room + every camera), and the room switches (VJ TOOLS, PORTALS SETUP DOME, TOOLS WHITEBOARD, setPiece neon, PANO off) light up the SHOWTIME! buttons.
- How: `SHOW_LINK` (chalk / panel / neon -> chalkboard.on, chatPanel.on, pieceOn('neon')); `streamer.setShowOpt` is wrapped so those keys set the room object, and `backdrop` keeps the dome in step (BACK: DOME = dome on; any other backdrop = dome off). `syncShowFromRoom()` copies room state into showOpt (writes only on a difference) on every `drawMixScreen` and in `saveDome`. All persist as before (vire.dome, vire.chalkboard, vire.chatPanel, settings.pieces, vire.showOpts).
- Kept stream-only on purpose: LED WALL (the flat VISUALS backdrop stands in for the 3D wall in the shot, #372), GEAR, ROBO-BODY, the GoPro prop.
- Tested in the PC pane: whiteboard and neon both ways, dome from BACK: DOME / next backdrop / setDome on and off.
- Backup: backups/pre-401/main.js. No server restart.

## #403 Cheaper, steadier dome (owner: flicker, passthrough when looking down with the dome on) (cly3dj-dev)
- Owner keeps the look: dome on + HEADSET CAM = a first-person shot, decks in front, dome (video pano) behind. Passthrough stays as it is (no session switch, no opaque clear). Only the dome's drawing changed (skybox.js).
- UVs from the sphere's own vertices (SphereGeometry equirect uv, u mirrored: vUv = (1 - uv.x, uv.y)) instead of atan / asin per pixel plus textureGrad with seam-fixed gradients. Flattening moves vertices along their own direction, so the mapping stays exact at the vertices (256 x 128 segments); hardware gradients are right since the geometry has its own seam column.
- Drawn last among opaque objects (renderOrder 1000, was -1000) and pinned just inside the far plane (gl_Position.z = 0.99999 w): early depth rejection skips every dome pixel already covered by gear, tablet, hands, walls. Nothing else relied on the dome being drawn first (only transparent or offscreen materials skip depth writes).
- Video panos (no mipmaps): footprint-adaptive sampling. Where a screen pixel covers more than 1.2 texels (far floor, grazing angles) four rotated-grid taps are averaged (stops the sparkle that reads as flicker); where the video is magnified (most of the view) one tap, full sharpness. Uniforms texSize, aa (1 for video). Stills unchanged: full resolution, trilinear mipmaps, anisotropy 4.
- PC check: old vs new renders of the neon room pano from the DJ's eye (ahead, looking down, turned round) identical. GPU timing on the RTX 4050 is too small and noisy to show the gain (dome ~0.2 ms at Quest eye size); the savings (per-pixel math, overdraw) count on the Quest's tile GPU. Untested in the headset.
- Not done (owner's call): dome writing real depth for the Quest's reprojection, an opaque clear behind the dome.
- Backup: backups/pre-403/skybox.js. No server restart.

## #404 DOME under HEADSET CAM, GoPro to hand (A), GoPro reset, DOME EXPOSURE (owner, 18 Oct) (cly3dj-dev)
- Owner: video panos sharper after #403, flicker only very rarely now.
- SHOWTIME! OBJECTS: DOME was greyed with HEADSET CAM (left over from before #401); now always on/off (it is the room's dome; dome + HEADSET CAM = the owner's first-person shot).
- A on the right controller (gamepad button 4, press edge, xr.js -> ctx.onA -> `gpSummon`): the GoPro that is the camera comes to that hand, 15 cm in front of the controller, 2 cm up, facing where it points (grab / hold / release on the prop, so it saves). Grip it from there to place it. Nothing happens without an active GoPro.
- GoPro reset: the double-tap window was 350 ms, but a tablet poke re-arms only after the tip leaves the screen by 2 cm, so it almost never fit. Now 700 ms, and a long press (0.6 s, `mixScreenLong` now runs a button's `long` on TOOLS pages) on GOPRO / GOPRO2 (STREAM and the SHOWTIME! camera row) = `goproReset`: back to its start spot, made the camera (source back to SHOWTIME! from HEADSET / PHONE CAM).
- DOME EXPOSURE on PORTALS SETUP (cell beside DOME SIZE): -3 .. +1 EV in 0.25 steps, saved as dome.ev in vire.dome; skybox.js multiplies the colour by 2^EV (uniform gain; also the full VR sky, same material). Clipped whites in the picture itself can only go grey, not get detail back. DOME TURN moved to its own row (landscape: third row, help beside it).
- Tested in the PC pane: exposure -1.5 EV -> gain 0.354, saved; GoPro2 brought from 2.4 m up to a test grip at hand height, then long-press reset put it back; PORTALS page in both orientations.
- Backup: backups/pre-404/ (main.js, xr.js, skybox.js). No server restart.
